A link removed and added again from now on starts as not read ("Added · first read within the hour") until its first read. Rows already there are as they were: a link removed before 0008 starts as not read when it is added again after it.

Owner's time: **about 1 minute.**

### 16.7 Undoing 0008

Not needed: the new answer is the old one plus `past`, and five items where there were none; the page reads both; and a link added again that starts as not read is read within the hour. If wanted, paste in one go 0006's `create function public.school_feed_status(p_kid uuid)` and `create function public.set_school_feed(p_kid uuid, p_kind text, p_url text)` blocks (from `supabase/migrations/0006_homework_school.sql`, each down to its `$$;`), each with `create or replace function` in place of `create function`, and
```sql
create or replace function public.hub_schema_version() returns int language sql immutable set search_path = '' as $$ select 7 $$;
```
(the same definition 0007 has). `create or replace` keeps the grants. The check is then 7's again (an all-past link shows the page's "already past" words), and a link added again keeps its old last read, as on 7. 0008 can be run again later as it is: it finds 7 and 0006's two functions, and replaces them again.

## 17. Later, checks during the build, and honest limits

### 17.1 Later (each additive)

- **Parents only** for homework (a tab and reads only admins see): a small change to the hub's core (`nav.admin`) and the policies.
- **Photo reads** of an assignment sheet by the AI (cropped in the browser, never stored), for Pat and the parents only.
- **Jordan asking the AI himself** (a split of one step from the stuck sheet), after a term of use, if the family wants it; with its own daily cap.
- **Jordan setting his own homework times** (rows by weekday, with the rule that a time set on its own day earns no on-time points that day).
- **Repeating lesson series**, lessons "with Pat" done in the tutoring session, a link on a lesson.
- **A note to Jordan on anything**, not only a milestone ("You finished the research two days early"): v1.3's coach's notes are on milestones only.
- **Pace in the bars and in placement** (0006, §5.4), together.
- **"Saving for"** one reward, with a bar.
- **Push notifications** (the end of a block on a locked phone, "time for homework"); a service worker for an offline start; an outbox for homework writes.
- **Several tutors for several kids** in one view (the table already allows 3 links per kid).
- **Homework time for Taylor to see** if she's in charge after school (she can already be along on the event).
- **Forwarded Blackbaud emails**; the **SKY API**, only if the school offers it.

**Not at all:** grades, scores or teacher comments as points; leaderboards, mascots, pets, streaks or taking points away as a penalty; a sign-in for Pat; assignments or steps on the wall; the AI writing, solving, outlining or summarizing any part of the work; an open chat with the AI; scraping Blackbaud with Jordan's password; any record of a medical or learning label.

### 17.2 Checks during the build (not settled by the research)

- **Anthropic:** the request over plain `fetch` (headers, `anthropic-version: 2026-10-21`, `output_config.format`, the fallback beta and `fallbacks: 'default'`, Opus 5.5's `allowed_fallback_models`, the spend-limit message, the 402 and credit-balance messages) against the current reference; real latency and token counts from **Test the AI planner**; the system prompt's token count (`count_tokens`); the retention period (its two pages disagree); tune the prompt on a comparison set of 10 made-up assignments (an essay, a test, a reading, a lab report, a short worksheet, one in Spanish, one asking it to write the essay, one that doesn't fit, one with a deadline inside it, a lesson), and try effort `low` there before keeping `medium`.
- **Supabase:** `EdgeRuntime.waitUntil` and the 400-second wall clock on this project's paid plan.
- **Placement cost:** `private.person_items` and `agenda` over 28 to 56 days inside Pat's read and the AI input, measured on the stand-ins and on the live project; if slow, read `agenda` once for the range.
- **`add_homework_time`'s start date** against `events_weekdays` for every weekday set, across the clock change.
- **The timer on the family's iPhones** (Safari and home-screen; on iOS 26 and later, where the My day icon opens as a web app by default): Wake Lock, the chime after the Start tap, the silent switch with and without **Chime even on silent**, coming back after 20 minutes locked; and on Android. **On a computer:** the chime and the notification in a hidden Chrome and Safari tab. **The alarm automation** (Shortcuts, Alarm, Is Stopped) on his iPhone.
- **The Spanish words** with a Spanish speaker.
- **The point values** after two weeks with Jordan (are 100–170 a fortnight and the reward prices right?), and the review with him after a term.
- **(0006) Blackbaud:** that a parent's login offers the feeds is confirmed (§1.3); the date meaning, the all-day form, the time zone and how stable the UIDs are (settled by the first import, and by the first real reads against `school.test.ts`'s fixture); whether a description carries anything but the teacher's text; a school on its own domain would need the host check widened on purpose; whether the setting must be turned on again each school year.
- **Pinned lists:** the anon count in `access.test.sql`, the realdb allowlists, `walkthrough.test.js`, the docs check's number words, CLAUDE.md "Client code", `cron-sql.test.ts` (0006).

### 17.3 Honest limits

- **Timers on phones:** no sound or pop-up when the phone is locked or in another app; sound only after the Start tap, and the silent switch may mute it; no vibration on iPhone; Wake Lock only on iOS 16.4 and later, and unreliable in home-screen mode on some versions. Points never depend on the phone's timer.
- **Points can be gamed a little:** the end buttons are self-report, a timer can be left running, and his own homework earns; whole minutes seen, the daily and weekly caps, the adults' view of every block and the parents' approval of every trade (with where the points came from) are the controls.
- **Timers on computers:** a page can show a notification only while it is open; a school-managed laptop isn't the place for his link.
- **Homework writes need a connection**, as task ticks on My day do.
- **Homework time is only as right as the event:** a field trip is a one-day change on the Calendar page, or steps land on a day he can't work (he taps Not today).
- **Placement counts days and minutes, not clock times.** Tonight is an ordered list for tonight's homework time, which keeps it one tap and survives a late start.
- **The AI guesses:** estimates can be short, it can misread the teacher's text, it never sees attachments, it can refuse. It only ever produces a draft that people edit, and every failure leaves a starter plan.
- **(0006) The feed** depends on two school switches, lags up to an hour (Blackbaud's, plus the hourly read), has an undocumented format, and may need the school to switch it on again each year. A link can't be cancelled at the school: Remove only stops the hub using it.

## 18. Where this design came from

Three drafts were written independently from four research notes (what helps kids start, plan and finish; what Blackbaud gives a family; the AI call; fit with the code), all kept outside the repository, and judged on: help for a kid with executive-function difficulties finishing a two-week assignment; the owner's four answers; privacy, safety and cost for a child and an outside tutor; fit with the code and its rules; being buildable and testable in one PR; and the owner's time. This design starts from the smallest-first draft, restaged into v1.3 (two PRs on one migration, the AI in the second) and 0006, and grafts:
- **From the smallest-first draft:** the data model (assignments and lessons in one table; steps only for plans people saved; one ledger); homework time as an ordinary repeating event; placement in SQL as the one rule for starter plans and AI drafts, with its buffer; `get_person_day` copied with marked lines, as 0004 did; `day.html?h=` for Pat (the confirmation-only mode is the precedent); the shared plan editor; no change to `digest_for`, `calendar_desired` or `day_text` in 0005; `v3db`/`v4db`; the AI flow, written so it could move into 0005 whole.
- **From the kid-first draft:** Jordan's working screen: **Now** with the next step, its "done looks like" line, his own start cue and one big Start; his start cue and if-stuck line typed by him; the stuck sheet (Make it smaller, Ask Pat, Next step) without AI; the end screen where **More to do** earns the same as **Step done**; milestones set with Pat, with plain steps his to move (and, after review, the draft's **Re-plan**, which the first merge had lost); "alone, with Pat, or with a parent" recorded only; no points without a step or a lesson; reward suggestions; Pat's view in a fixed order; the phone-alarm README line; 0006's "New from school", schedule feeds as busy time, his own drafts and the AI's "What's missing?".
- **From the trust-first draft:** the AI off until a parent turns it on; **What Claude will see** and **Send to Claude**; no photos (Live Text); `tutor_links` with an end date and a "last used" time; caps per day, per link, per assignment and per month, with the Console limit; busy time subtracted in the first release; on-time points paid at the answer and milestone points paid at the check (its 80% rule was replaced after review by whole minutes seen, §19); trades asked by Jordan and decided on Today; a parent's void with a reason instead of a clawback; the "who sees what" line; Erase; the switches card and the off-lines; retention; the label-word test; 0006's strict address check, the private address table and the hidden first import.
- **This design's own choice:** **no SDK and no second Edge Function.** `homework.ts` calls the Messages API over HTTPS with the injected `fetch`, as `google.ts` calls Google, so `hub-jobs` stays free of outside imports, v1.1's one-function decision and the docs check's secret scan hold, and the email never loads an SDK. A separate `homework-ai` function with the SDK is the fallback only if this hits a real blocker.
- **Turned down for v1.3:** Jordan's link reaching the AI (the owner said AI *with the tutor helping*; Anthropic's extra duties are for minors using it directly; a leaked My day link would spend money); photo reads; a "timeout credit" that paid for blocks nobody ended (a block is paid when its ring runs out on his page, or when his page next sees it within 24 hours: never with no page at all); 120 points a day (hard to lower later; **1 point a minute was turned down here first, then chosen by the owner** after the first build, with a cap of 60 a day, §1.2 decision 2); a `get_person_day` wrapper; Jordan setting his own homework times; six AI kinds; a second Edge Function; a third public page (`plan.html`); parents-only row-level security with a core change (now an owner decision); a nightly clean-up job; a typed note required on every block; a separate migration for the AI (§19). Most are in §17.1.

## 19. Review changes

Three adversarial reviews (privacy and safety; Jordan's experience; technical and operations) read this design. Each finding was checked against the doc, the code at `aced5bb` and, for Anthropic's API, the bundled API reference (its model table cached 2026-10-06). All 47 held up at least in part: 43 are applied as proposed or with a simpler equivalent, 4 in part, none rejected.

| Finding | Outcome | Why, in one line |
|---|---|---|
| P1 (privacy, major): Pat's link can mint unlimited milestone points | Applied | Confirmed (a link can add a lesson, plan four milestones, give and check it, and milestones were outside every cap): a weekly milestone cap (80), the trade line shows the balance and where this week's points came from, and the README sends Pat to Pat's own device (§8.1, §9.3, §13.1). |
| P2 (privacy, major): Pat's link is a live view of when he works and whether he is alone | Applied | Pat's view loses `session`, and `with` becomes `with_tutor`; nothing live at all, which also covers UX-M4's narrower "only while With Pat" (§3.4, §9.5, §13.2). |
| P3 (privacy, major): "no names" isn't true by default and the preview folds away | Applied | The AI can't be turned on without a never-send word (a trigger), titled names and each part of a tutor's label are scrubbed, the preview opens whenever what would be sent changed (Ask again included), and "no names" became "the names Home Hub knows taken out" (§6.4, §6.12, §9.2). |
| P4 (privacy): the consent line understates what is sent and kept; the fallback model is unknown | In part | The line now lists everything sent and the 2-year retention of flagged requests; naming Claude Sonnet 5.5 as the fallback is not possible (the array form takes only models in Opus 5.5's `allowed_fallback_models`, Claude Opus 5 and Opus 4.8, neither a 30-day-retention model), so `'default'` stays with its targets named and checked at build (§6.5, §13.4). |
| P5 (privacy): the leaked-link cost bound counts one call; anonymous calls take the household lock | Applied | Who is checked before the lock (a malformed token never reaches SQL), only 429 and 529 are retried, and the bound is recomputed with the fallback (about $6 a day for a link; the $15 monthly limit is the real stop) (§6.2, §6.3, §6.5, §6.11). |
| P6 (privacy): a teacher's text can close its tag and pose as a parent's request | Applied | Every `<` and `>` in a field becomes `‹` `›` before it is wrapped, and the preview shows the same (§6.4). |
| P7 (privacy, 0006): the feed link can leak through fetch errors; redirects and descriptions under-specified | Applied | Fetch exceptions become fixed codes and `message(err)` is never used on a feed; every redirect passes the address check, at most 3; the feed's description isn't kept (the simpler of the two options) (§11.3, §12). |
| P8 (privacy): "who sees what" doesn't match the grants | Applied | Confirmed (members read every homework table): the teacher's text and drafts are "for the grown-ups who sign in", and his who-sees line says "the grown-ups in your family" (§4.1, §6.1, §9.7). |
| P9 (privacy): old homework records are kept for ever | Applied | Done assignments, sessions and decided asks go after 180 days; ledger rows stay with their links set null, so the balance doesn't move (§13.3, owner decision 13). |
| P10 (privacy): Ask Pat keeps no history and parents see only open questions | Applied | `qa_log` keeps earlier pairs, an answer can be changed by its author for 15 minutes and then only cleared by a parent, and the parents' page and Today show answers and notes (§4.5, §9.1, §9.3). |
| P11 (privacy): the daily cap of 30 is twice an honest day | Applied | The cap is 20, against an honest day of about 13 (§8.1, owner decision 2). |
| P12 (privacy): `short_name` can give Pat Jordan's surname | Applied | Confirmed (`0002:1148-1156`): Pat's view uses the first word of his name (§3.3, §3.4). |
| UX-M1 (kid, major): his real homework never reaches his screen, and he can't put it there | Applied | A seventh token function, `assignment_by_token` (add, Quick, drop), so he writes down his own homework and a small one is tonight's Now in one tap; nineteen anon functions; 0006's Quick places the same way (§3.5, §4.1, §11.4). |
| UX-M2 (kid, major): finishing early earns nothing | Applied | Confirmed (the 80% rule plus Done): points are 1 per 5 whole minutes seen whatever the answer (since the owner's change, 1 a minute, §8.1), the on-time +5 needs 8 minutes (now +10 at 5), and Done offers Keep going in the same block (§7.1, §8.1). |
| UX-M3 (kid, major): no way back when he falls behind | Applied | The kid-first draft's Re-plan is restored, earlier steps fold into one line, a passed milestone goes to "To talk about" with Pat, the card's Next equals Now, and "promises" became "checkpoints" (§4.5, §9.5, §9.6). |
| UX-M4 (kid, major): the adults' buttons are verdicts with no words | Applied | Looks done takes a note and Not yet needs one, both shown to him and the parents; Today counts questions; Pat's view opens with "Start with what he did. Ask what's next and when he'll start it."; the live view went with P2 (§4.5, §9.3, §9.5). |
| UX-M5 (kid, major): the phone on the desk with notifications, and no laptop | Applied | The README schedules a Focus or Do Not Disturb for homework time, offers a family computer window and warns off a school-managed laptop; the chime is scheduled on the audio clock and a desktop notification is offered; tests run at 1280 and 420px (§7.2, §15). |
| UX-m1 (kid): two taps to the ring; the alarm can't open it | Applied | The card has its own Start; the alarm automation is an optional README line to check on his phone (Apple's page couldn't be opened from the review's session) (§9.6, §15). |
| UX-m2 (kid): on time ignores busy time at the start | Applied | The window is around the first free minute of homework time (§7.1). |
| UX-m3 (kid): "+9 today of 30" reads as a target | Applied | He sees "+9 today"; the limit shows only when it is hit; the parents keep "of 20" (§9.1, §9.6). |
| UX-m4 (kid): "your estimates" is false, and the bars ignore pace | In part | The words are now "the planned time"; the bars stay at plain minutes until 0006 puts pace into the bars and the placement together, so they never disagree (§5.4, §5.5). |
| UX-m5 (kid): his who-sees line leaves out Sam | Applied | Same fix as P8. |
| UX-m6 (kid): every reward is days away | Applied | Rewards start in three sizes (about 15, 60 and 150; since the owner's change 15 instant, 200 and 500), and his screen says "Tell a parent you asked." (§1.2, §8.5). |
| UX-m7 (kid): the stretch question never reaches him | Applied | `assignments.stretch`, copied by Use this plan, shows under Due soon and earns nothing (§3.2, §6.10, §9.6). |
| UX-m8 (kid): the starter plans break the step rule | Applied | Every starter step has a verb, a product and a "done looks like", and their minutes are cut to his first length; a test checks them (§4.6). |
| UX-m9 (kid): iPhone sound and the home-screen icon | In part | An optional, off-by-default **Chime even on silent** where `navigator.audioSession` exists, and the iOS 26 home-screen default as a build check; the WebKit threads and the iOS 26 report couldn't be confirmed here, so neither is relied on (§7.2, §17.2). |
| UX-m10 (kid): points have no planned end | Applied | Decision 2 and §8.4: the family reviews the values with him after a term. |
| UX-m11 (kid): Make it smaller leaves the timer on the big step | Applied | Confirmed: the running block moves to the new step in the same call (§4.5). |
| T-M1 (tech, major): a run that dies stays "running" for ever | Applied | `private.ai_sweep` in every start and trim, a finish after the lease answers `late`, the views read a stale run as `timeout`, the poll lasts the lease, and the background work never throws (§6.2, §6.3, §6.8). |
| T-M2 (tech, major): `agenda`'s 62-day limit breaks both views | Applied | Confirmed (`0002:964-965`): free minutes stop at 56 days, placement has an 8-week horizon, and the views never raise (`no_time`) (§3.4, §5.3, §5.4). |
| T-M3 (tech, major): points booked on the answer's day | Applied | `private.award` takes `p_day`, the block's start day, for the cap and the on-time entry (§3.5, §8.1). |
| T-M4 (tech, major): stage v1.3 as two PRs with their own migrations | In part | Two PRs, but on one migration: the AI's risks are in the Edge Function, Anthropic and the owner's account, not its SQL, which PR A's database suite tests; this keeps one paste for the owner and the numbering of 0006 (§0, §2, §16). |
| T-M5 (tech, major, 0006): 500 events can't hold a 14-month feed | Applied | Every event is parsed, only the hub's window is kept, then the caps apply (400 and 2,000), with a `too_many` stop (§11.3). |
| T-m1 (tech): the never-send CHECK fails the docs check | Applied | Confirmed by running the reviewer's copy of the check; the CHECK became a household trigger, and the copy now finds no mismatch (§3.2, §15). |
| T-m2 (tech): Test can't run before the switch is on | Applied | `test` runs while the AI is off, and the switch is drawn only after a Working test (§6.3, §6.12). |
| T-m3 (tech): the smoke check and "no AI button" can't work | Applied | The smoke check sends a test and expects `not_set_up`; `not_set_up` answers with words, since SQL can't see the secret (§6.9, §16.2). |
| T-m4 (tech): running out of credit shows "Try again later" for ever | Applied | Confirmed (402 `billing_error` in the API reference): a `billing` stop with its own words, and auto-reload in the setup (§6.5, §6.9, §6.12). |
| T-m5 (tech): the worst case leaves out fallbacks and retries; cost reads the wrong usage | Applied | `cents` sums `usage.iterations` at each model's price, no retry after a 5xx or a timeout, and the worst run is about 60 cents (§6.5, §6.11). |
| T-m6 (tech): `last_used_at` bumps `updated_at` | Applied | Confirmed (`private.stamp`, `0002:367-370`): the update trigger skips that column (§3.2). |
| T-m7 (tech): writes from the links aren't repeat-safe | Applied | A simpler equivalent of v1.2's key table: added rows take their id from the page, Not today names its day, decisions answer as they are, and `p_rev` is required (§3.2, §3.5, §4.4). |
| T-m8 (tech): ids and keys from the page aren't tied to the sender | Applied | Another kid's block id or another asker's key gets the same answer as an unknown one (§6.3, §7.1). |
| T-m9 (tech): the scrub misses "Pat" | Applied | Same fix as P3. |
| T-m10 (tech): Pat's 3-second poll recomputes the heaviest answer | Applied | Free minutes are computed once per answer, and the poll asks `p_part 'ai'` every 5 seconds (§3.5, §9.5). |
| T-m11 (tech): five specs don't do what they say | Applied | The day rules are `plan_save`'s; the preview takes the kind and answers and equals `input.message`; ISO weekdays; the steps' `updated_*` set by the functions; the views never raise (§3.2, §3.5). |
| T-m12 (tech): U+2028 escapes broke an MCP deploy before | Applied | Confirmed (`util.ts:27-31`): strings go through `oneLine`, bidi and zero-width characters are SQL's, and a test greps the source (§6.8, §14). |
| T-m13 (tech): a repeated ref isn't covered | Applied | The first copy is kept, later ones dropped, and `plan_save` refuses two entries with one id (§4.4, §6.8). |
| T-m14 (tech, 0006): parsing misses cases the research warned about | Applied | Exclusive all-day ends, repeated keys, `RRULE`, unknown `TZID`s, items aging out and moved dates without a UID, each tested (§11.3, §12). |

## 20. 0007: the coach view (`0007_homework_coach.sql`, schema 7)

Pat Lee is Jordan's tutor **and** his executive-function coach. The owner explained this, and was told (without objection) that Pat's link would also show, beyond the homework it shows today: his points and rewards, his focus sessions, his own calendar and his daily tasks, and never the rest of the family. Owner decision 16 (§1.2): **coach view off by default; turn it on for the tutor.** It is one migration and its pages: no Edge Function and no `cron.sql` change.

What it is not: a way for Pat to change anything but a note for the parents (no writes to points, rewards, tasks or events), a view of anyone else, or anything live.

### 20.1 Guard and version

The guard refuses to run without 0006 (`hub_schema_version() < 6`: "The school feed (migration 0006) isn't installed"), twice ("already installed"), on a database set back to 6 ("set back to 6", §20.7), or on one that isn't as 0006 left it (`tutor_links` with no `coach` column, and none of the three columns below on `task_log`, `tasks` and `events`; `private.homework_view_tutor`, `private.homework_view_kid` and their `_v5` bodies, `private.school_marks`, `private.school_new`, `private.tutor_link_by_token`, `private.count_homework`, `private.points_balance`, `public.agenda` and `private.google_for` there). It ends with `hub_schema_version()` returning 7. One transaction, additive, no rows seeded; every rule of §3 applies (row-level security, `set search_path = ''`, revoke then grant).

### 20.2 Tables and columns

```
tutor_links.coach  boolean not null default false     -- the coach view; only the parents set it (set_tutor_coach)

coach_notes (a note the coach leaves for the parents, from the tutor link)
  id         uuid PK                       -- the page's key: a resend finds it
  link_id    uuid not null → tutor_links on delete cascade (so Erase takes them)
  note       text not null, 1 to 500 characters, cleaned by private.clean_block (line breaks kept)
  written_by text not null                 -- "<the link's label> (tutor link)", as the link's other writes
  created_at timestamptz not null          -- private.now()
  done_at, done_by                         -- a parent's Done (both or neither)
  index coach_notes_link on (link_id, created_at desc)
  RLS: admins select ("Parents can see the coach's notes"); revoke all from public, anon, authenticated, then
  grant select to authenticated. No write grant: coach_note_by_token writes, coach_note_done marks done.

Who was in charge (so a chore or a series handed to him doesn't bring someone else's days; §20.3):
task_log.responsible_id      uuid → people on delete set null   -- the task's person in charge as the entry is written
tasks.responsible_changed_at timestamptz                        -- when its person in charge last changed
events.people_changed_at     timestamptz                        -- when its person in charge (or, with nobody in charge,
                                                                --   its people, as a set) last changed
```

Three triggers keep them, so no page and no function writes them: `task_log_in_charge` (before insert on `task_log`; SECURITY DEFINER, so the stamp never depends on who writes the entry: the definer functions, the daily job) sets the entry's `responsible_id` to its task's, whatever it is given; `tasks_in_charge_since` and `events_people_since` (before update) set the time to `private.now()` when the person in charge changes (for an event with nobody in charge before and after, when its people do) and otherwise keep the time the row had, whatever the save sends, so a page can't clear it. A new task or event keeps what it is given: none, or the time of a row a page puts back with Undo after deleting it. The entries already there have no stamp, and the tasks and events already there take 0007's time (a default dropped at once, so no row is saved and nothing else changes): nothing from before 0007 is taken for his.

`tutor_links` keeps its grants: the parents read it (row-level security), nobody updates it directly; `set_tutor_coach` is the only way to change `coach`, and the stamp trigger stamps the change. **New link**, **Off**, **On**, **Extend** and a new name keep the switch as it is.

### 20.3 What the coach sees (`private.coach_view(p_link)`)

```
{ language, as_of, today, kid: 'Jordan',                       -- the link's language; his first name only
  points: { balance,                                            -- all his ledger lines
            this_week: { focus, on_time, milestones, given, voided, trades },   -- the household week, Mon–Sun
            last_week: { … the same },                          -- milestones: both halves (milestone_done, milestone)
            lines: [{ day, kind, delta, note }] } | null,       -- the last 30 days (today − 29 to today), newest first, at
                                                                --   most 300; the note he sees on #points, never who
  rewards: [{ title, cost, instant }] | null,                  -- his active ones
  asks: [{ title, cost, status, asked_on, decided_on }] | null,  -- waiting, and asked or decided in the last 30 days (at most
                                                                --   100), newest first by the later of the two; household
                                                                --   dates; never who decided, nor a parent's note
  focus: [{ day, assignment, minutes, seen_minutes, result, note }],   -- finished blocks that started in the last 30 days,
                                                                --   newest first; assignment: its title (through the step
                                                                --   when the block has none of its own; null for his own block)
  calendar: [{ day, end_day, all_day, starts, ends, title }],   -- today − 7 to today + 13
  tasks: [{ day, title, status }],                              -- his late ones first (due before today − 7, oldest first),
                                                                --   then today − 7 to today; status late, open, done,
                                                                --   skipped or missed
  notes: [{ id, note, day }] }                                  -- this link's own notes of the last 30 days, newest first
```

With points off, `points`, `rewards` and `asks` are null, as on his My day. Null for a link whose coach view is off.

**His own calendar.** The hub models who is on an item as one person in charge (`responsible_id`) and the people with them (`participant_ids`), per day: a one-day change can name someone else in charge, nobody in charge, or other people (`event_changes`), and `public.agenda(p_from, p_to)` gives each day's version. "His own" is: events of that day, not cancelled, that **he is in charge of**, or that have **nobody in charge and him as the only one on them**; and the Google events `private.google_for(p_person, p_from, p_to, p_grown_up)` reaches him with that are in his charge (his own person calendar, and the pets' or the house's if he is their owner), never the Family calendar's (which are the family's). So "Dinner out" that Sam is in charge of and he is along on, a movie night with another kid and nobody in charge, and Sam's weekly class he goes to are not his, except on a day the class's change puts him in charge. `agenda` gives every day of a series today's people, so **a day that began before the series last changed hands** (`events.people_changed_at`; an all-day one begins at midnight) is left out, unless that day's own change put him in charge or named its people with nobody in charge: Sam's weekly physio handed to him today shows its coming days, not Sam's of last Monday. Project jobs aren't calendar items here, and tasks are below. Each item: its day and end day, all day or its times, and its title; a private one is "Private item" (`private.word(p_key, p_lang)`, in the link's language). Never who else is on it, the place, the notes, the directory entry or the day's note. The last 7 days and the next 14 (today included): today − 7 to today + 13.

**His daily tasks.** What his My day lists as his, of the tasks he is in charge of now: the open ones over today − 7 to today on the day they are due (`public.agenda(p_from, p_to)`, by `private.current_due(t)`), and before that his **late** ones (open, not paused, due before today − 7, as his My day's Late has them; status `late`, on their due day, first and oldest first). Of their history, only what the hub wrote **while he was in charge** (`task_log.responsible_id`): a done one-off on its due day (the entry its tick wrote), the done and skipped days of repeating ones, and the days the daily job logged **missed**, for a day after the task last changed hands (`tasks.responsible_changed_at`): the job logs a missed day when the next one comes, so a weekly chore Sam missed and a parent then handed to him is logged after the hand-over, and is Sam's. So a chore rotated to him brings none of the other person's days (done, skipped or missed), nor the other person's done one-off; an entry from before 0007 has no stamp and isn't shown. A private one, or a **health** task, is "Private item", with its day and status, as on the wall (v1.1 rule 7b: pills and appointments don't go anywhere outside the family by name).

**Nothing of anyone else.** No name but his first name, no email, phone number, person's id or token, no other person's item, no dinners, shopping list, directory or other kids. Free text the family typed is shown as typed, as Pat's link already shows step titles: his own events' and tasks' titles, reward titles, a parent's words with given points or a correction (which Jordan sees on #points). The hub's own records of who did what (`who`, `done_by`, `decided_by`, emails) never are.

### 20.4 Functions

**The tutor link** (SECURITY DEFINER; revoke from public, service_role; grant to anon, authenticated). Each starts `l := private.tutor_link_by_token(p_token)` (an unknown, malformed or replaced token, a link that is off or past its end date, or a kid whose homework is off or who is no longer active: null) and answers null too when `l.coach` is off.
- `coach_view_by_token(p_token text) returns jsonb`: the coach view (§20.3). Not counted; it notes `last_used_at` as `tutor_view_by_token` does.
- `coach_note_by_token(p_token text, p_key uuid, p_note text) returns jsonb`: a note for the parents, with the page's key: a resend (the same key) changes nothing, isn't counted and answers `changed: false`; another link's key → 42501 "That note isn't on this link."; over 500 characters → 22023 "That's too long."; nothing left once cleaned → 22023 "Write a note first."; no key → 22023 "A note needs its id.". Counted as one of the link's writes (`private.count_homework(p_actor, p_what, p_max)`, 200 a day), and at most 5 notes a day for a link (P0001 "That's a lot for one day."). The link's notes older than 180 days go as it writes. Answers `{note_id, changed}` and the coach view.

**The parents** (SECURITY DEFINER; revoke from public, anon, service_role; grant to authenticated; 42501 "Only a parent can change homework." unless `private.is_admin()`):
- `set_tutor_coach(p_link uuid, p_coach boolean) returns void`: on or off for one link, at once. 22023 "That link is gone." for an unknown one, "Choose on or off." for null.
- `coach_note_done(p_note uuid) returns void`: Done on a note; a second Done changes nothing; 22023 "That note is gone." for an unknown one.

**Private:** `private.coach_view(p_link uuid) returns jsonb` (STABLE; revoked from public, anon, authenticated: only the two definer functions call it). The three trigger functions of §20.2, `private.task_log_in_charge()` (SECURITY DEFINER), `private.tasks_in_charge_since()` and `private.events_people_since()`, each `returns trigger`; revoked from public, anon, authenticated (a trigger runs whoever writes the row).

**Replaced**, with the same signatures (`create or replace`, so they keep their grants), each 0006's wrapper with one marked part; 0005's `_v5` bodies aren't touched:
- `private.homework_view_tutor(p_link uuid, p_assignment uuid, p_part text)`: with the coach view on, `coach: true` (not for `p_part` `'ai'`). The page reads `coach_view_by_token` only when it sees it.
- `private.homework_view_kid(p_kid uuid, p_lang text)`: when a tutor link of his (active, not past its end date) has the coach view on, `me.coach`, the first word of that link's label (the first such link), so his screen can say what that link sees too.
- `hub_schema_version()` returns 7.

With the coach view off on every link, as 0007 leaves them, every answer is 0006's (`tests/db/run.sh` 5d and 5e, with 0007 applied); turning it on for a link adds exactly `coach: true` to its view and `me.coach` to his (5f). With 0007, anon can call **twenty-two** token functions (§3.6).

### 20.5 The parents' Homework page

On schema 7, each tutor link in **Switches → Tutor links** (the parents only) has:
- **Coach view**, a switch, off by default, with exactly what it shows: "Also shows Pat Jordan's points and rewards (this week's and last week's points, and the last 30 days), his focus sessions, his own calendar (the next 14 days and the last 7) and his daily tasks, read-only, and lets Pat leave you a note here. Private items and health tasks show as “Private item”. Never the rest of the family: no one else's events, tasks or names, no dinners, shopping list or contacts." (`set_tutor_coach`; "Coach view on for Pat Lee, tutor".)
- **Notes from Pat Lee, tutor (n)**: the notes not done, newest first ("Yesterday 6:30pm · “Suggest lowering the on-time bonus.”"), each with **Done** (`coach_note_done`); the done ones of the last 30 days folded under **Earlier notes**.

It reads `.from('coach_notes')` for the kid's links, on schema 7 only and for the parents only. Sam gets no row and no request (the links are the parents'); on schema 6 nothing of it shows and nothing is asked.

### 20.6 Pat's link and Jordan's screen

- **Pat's link** (`day.html?h=`): when the link's answer says `coach: true`, a **Coach** section under the fixed ones ("Jordan's family lets you see his points, rewards, focus sessions, his own calendar and his daily tasks. Read-only."), on the list's screen only, read with `coach_view_by_token` after each read of the list (on open, on `focus` and `visibilitychange`), but not while a note is being typed: **Points** (the balance; this week and last week by kind, in a small table; the last 30 days' lines, folded), **Rewards** and **Asks and cash-ins**, **Focus sessions**, **His calendar** (Next 14 days, Last 7 days), **His daily tasks** (his late ones first, "Late since Thu Oct 22", then "Not done yet", "Done", "Skipped", "Missed"), and **A note for Jordan's family** with **Send to the family** (`coach_note_by_token` with the page's key, kept until an answer and tied to the words it was made for: sent again as they are after "Can't reach Home Hub", it finds the note; changed first, they are a new note with a new key, and the first one, if it landed, stays a note of its own; the button is off while it is on its way; "Sent to the family."), then **Your notes to the family**. Day.js draws it in a box of its own under `plan.js`'s list. A null answer (turned off a moment ago) hides it; so does the next read of the list without `coach`. English and Spanish, as the link. Nothing is kept on the device.
- **Jordan's #homework**: with `me.coach` in his view, the who-sees line names everything the coach view shows: "Pat and the grown-ups in your family see your plan and your notes. The grown-ups and Pat see your points and rewards, your focus sessions, your own calendar and your daily tasks." (Spanish to match.)

### 20.7 Deploy order and undoing

No Edge Function and no `cron.sql` change, so v1.2's order applies: the site, then the migration.
1. `tests/run.sh` ends with `0 failed`. Merge. The site goes live on schema 6 and nothing changes: the Homework page shows no switch and asks for no notes, and Pat's link asks for no coach view (its answer never says `coach: true` before 0007).
2. **Owner, about 2 minutes:** paste `supabase/migrations/0007_homework_coach.sql` → **Run**. "isn't installed" means 0006 is missing; "already installed" means it's done; either way nothing was changed. Every link's coach view is off.
3. **Owner, about 1 minute (decision 16):** Homework → Switches → Tutor links → **Pat Lee, tutor** → **Coach view** on. Tell Pat (the Coach section shows the next time the link is opened) and Jordan (his screen says what Pat now sees).

Owner's time: **about 3 minutes.** To stop it for a link: **Coach view** off, at once. To hide it altogether, turn it off first, then paste in one go:
```sql
update public.tutor_links set coach = false;
create or replace function public.hub_schema_version() returns int language sql immutable set search_path = '' as $$ select 6 $$;
```
(the same definition 0006 has, with 6). Running 0007 again then says "set back to 6"; switching back is the same `create or replace` with 7. The notes stay until Erase.

### 20.8 Security and privacy

1. **Off by default, per link, the parents only.** A link a parent didn't turn on reads exactly what it read on 6; a second tutor's link needs its own switch.
2. **What a leaked link now shows** (with the coach view on): his points and their sources, his rewards and asks, three weeks of his own calendar's titles and times, a week of his task titles and his late ones (health tasks, like private ones, only as "Private item"), and what he worked on for a month. Never anyone else's, nor another person's days on a task or a series handed to him. The controls are the tutor link's: **Off** and **New link** stop it at once, the end date (180 days), "last used", and the parents can turn the coach view off without stopping the link.
3. **Nothing live** still holds: finished blocks only, with their day but no clock time and never with whom; no block running now.
4. **Writes:** only the note, cleaned, at most 500 characters, 5 a day for a link and counted with its 200 writes a day, labelled "<label> (tutor link)", kept 180 days, read only by the parents. Reads aren't counted, as the tutor's view isn't.
5. **No labels**: the coach view adds no field that could carry one, and the label-word check covers the new files.
6. **Accepted:** free text the family typed (his own events' and tasks' titles, reward titles, a parent's words with points) is shown as typed, as on Pat's link today; a title that names someone ("Piano with Taylor", his own event) shows that name. Events have no category, so a health appointment of his shows by its title unless it is private. The pets' and the house's Google calendars follow their owner now (as `google_for` has them): handed to him, their last 7 days show (they are the pets' and the house's, not a person's).
7. **Whose days:** a day of a task or a series counts as his only when the hub knows it was (§20.2, §20.3); what it can't know (before 0007, a missed day of a task handed to him on that day) is left out.

### 20.9 Tests

- **db** — `tests/db/coach.test.sql` (new; `fixtures.sql`, where the kid is Taylor, plus Robin, a second kid with his own homework, points and tutor link): the switch (off for every link, new or old; only a parent sets it; no column grant); coach off (the tutor's view and his view exactly as on 6, the two functions null, nothing written or counted); coach on (`coach: true` and nothing else new; the AI poll unchanged; exactly the keys of §20.3, and no other key anywhere; the balance, both weeks by kind, the lines; rewards and asks; focus sessions, never a running one; his calendar with tempting overlaps: a family dinner he is along on, a movie night with Robin, Sam's weekly class except the day its change puts him in charge or makes him its only one, a cancelled day, a directory entry with a phone number, a place and notes, private ones, two-day all-day ones, his own Google calendar and not Alex's or the Family calendar; his tasks, done, open, skipped, missed and private, not Sam's or Robin's; chores handed to him from Sam (daily, weekly, a one-off: his days since, none of Sam's, nor a day Sam missed that the job logged after) and series handed to him (only what began after, and a day its own change gave him), the three stamps and their triggers (an entry takes its task's person whatever it is given, a save can't clear the times, an entry with no stamp doesn't show); a health task as "Private item"; his late tasks first; an ask decided yesterday that he made 40 days ago; nothing of anyone else: no name, email, phone, id, dinner or list line; a Spanish link; points off; Robin's link sees only Robin); the notes (kept cleaned with who and when, counted, a resend, another link's key, too long, empty, no key, 5 a day, the next day, 180 days; the parents read and mark done, Sam and a stranger read none, nobody writes directly); the link's rules (Off, the end date, New link, homework off, a kid no longer active); Pat's token opens no My day and his My day token no coach view; privileges, definers and search_path; version 7; Erase. `tests/db/run.sh`: 0007's refusals (twice, on v1, at 0004, at 0005, set back to 6), its time on the tasks and events already there (without saving them) and no stamp on the entries already there, and 5f; 5d and 5e hold with 0007 applied. `access.test.sql`: version 7, twenty-two token functions, the definer list, the private helper, the function matrix.
- **e2e** — `tutor.test.js`: the Coach section (only with the switch on; read with `coach_view_by_token` and nothing else new; after the fixed sections; every part; private as "Private item"; his late tasks first, "Late since …"; an old ask decided yesterday; nothing of the family; the note checked, sent with the page's key, the button off while it goes, kept through a refresh; after a lost answer, sent again as it is with the same key, or changed with a new one; not on an assignment's screen; gone when the parents turn it off; points off; schema 7 with it off asks nothing; Spanish at 390px). `homework.test.js`: the switch and its words, `set_tutor_coach` on and off, the notes with Done and Earlier notes; none of it on schema 6 or for Sam; a phone. `day.test.js`: his who-sees line with the coach view on, naming what Pat sees, in English and Spanish. `homework.test.js`'s switch words say private items and health tasks show as "Private item".
- **realdb** — `access.test.js`: with the coach view off nothing new; Sam can't turn it on; on, signed out with Pat's token: exactly its keys, his things only, no one else's name, email, id, token, event, task, dinner or list line; the note, read only by the parents; no table gives rows signed out, every other anonymous function answers Pat's token with nothing, and his My day token opens no coach function; off again as before. `v1db` to `v4db` and `school.test.js` (schema 5): no page asks for the coach's table or functions.
- **docs** — this section's signatures, v2-design §12 (the table and column as 0007 has them, every signature), CLAUDE.md, the README's setup steps, twenty-two anon functions.

## 21. Timelines (no migration)

The owner, on Jordan's long assignments: "Some assignments are 2 weeks long and he would need a gantt type chart for those." Jordan is a short-term thinker who needs to **see time, the next step and the next win**. The owner chose both: an **overview** of all his open assignments as bars over the next 3 weeks, and inside each assignment a **chart of its steps** grouped by milestone across its days. The parents and Pat get the full drawings, with every step; Jordan gets a **simple path**: the same weeks as a row of days, flags on his milestones, today highlighted, and one line on top saying what the next flag is and when.

It is pages only: **no migration, no Edge Function, no `cron.sql` change**, and no new request. Every drawing is made from an answer each page already reads (§3.4). `?v=` is `20261031h` on every script and stylesheet of both pages.

### 21.1 What each page shows

- **The parents' Homework page** (`#/homework`; Sam too): **Next 3 weeks**, a section between Assignments and lessons and This week, with the overview of every open assignment and lesson (a plan given or not: the parents see every plan), each row a link to `#/homework/<id>`; left out when none is open. On `#/homework/<id>`, the assignment's **chart** above the plan editor, from the plan as saved (drawn again after each read, so after a save), with the days without homework time shaded from `homework_week`; none for a plan on fewer than 2 days, or none. From the rows the page reads (`assignments`, `homework_steps`, through `plan.js`'s `fromRows`).
- **Pat's link** (`day.html?h=`): **Next 3 weeks** at the top of the Assignments section (inside it, under its heading, so the fixed order of §9.5 doesn't change), each row a link to `#a/<id>`; none when nothing is open. On an assignment's screen, its **chart** between the fields and the editor, from the assignment's steps (`tutor_view_by_token` with `p_assignment`) and his two weeks on the link (`week`), drawn again after each read and each save.
- **Jordan's My day** (`day.html?t=`): on `#homework`, under Tonight (so tonight stays first) and before Due soon, **Your path** ("Tu camino"): a card for each plan **given to him** that is **due 4 or more days out or has a milestone**, by due date, at most 3, the rest folded under "+2 more" ("+2 más"); each card's name ("Essay: a river's journey ›") opens its plan, `#plan/<id>`, where **the path is at the top**. From `homework_by_token`'s `assignments` (their `steps` once given), drawn again with every read and every write's answer.

What Jordan's path says, with today Mon Nov 2:
- "Next flag: Outline · Thu (3 days)" (the next milestone not done and its day: "today", "tomorrow", a weekday with its days, or "in 9 days"; one whose day has passed, "from yesterday" or by its date, "from Fri Oct 30", on today's dot: a weekday alone would read as the coming one, which the dots show), then the dots: "Today", T, W, T … to the due date's star; past 14 dots, "… due Fri Nov 20".
- "Every flag done! Due Fri Nov 20 (18 days)" once every milestone is done; "Due Fri (4 days)" for a plan with none. A lesson is never "due": "Do by Sun (6 days)", "… do by Wed Nov 25".
- In Spanish: "Siguiente bandera: Outline · el jue (3 días)", "… · del vie 30 oct", "¡Todas las banderas hechas! Se entrega el vie 13 nov (11 días)", "Se entrega el vie (4 días)", a lesson "Hacer para el dom (6 días)", "Hoy".

### 21.2 The functions (`assets/plan.js`)

Three pure functions, each returning an HTML string (every typed text escaped; `''` when there is nothing to draw), with their words in English and Spanish in `WORDS` (the `tl_*` keys), no database call, runnable in Node's `vm` like the rest of `plan.js`, and listed in its header:
- `overview(items, o)`: `items` `[{ id, kind, title, subject, due_date, status, steps, steps_done?, next_milestone? }]`, `steps` either the steps (`[{ kind, day, done }]`) or, on Pat's list, their count with `steps_done`; `o` `{ today, days (21), lang, link(id) }`.
- `chart(a, o)`: `a` `{ kind, status, due_date, steps: [{ kind, title, minutes, day, done }] }`; `o` `{ today, lang, week }`, `week` as `homeworkDays` reads it (`homework_week`'s rows or Pat's `week`).
- `tlScroll(root)`: the page calls it right after it draws a chart into `root` (and only when it drew it again, so a box someone scrolled stays put): each chart wider than its box scrolls to the column its HTML names (`data-at`, §21.3).
- `path(a, o)`: `a` his view's assignment (`{ kind, status, due_date, steps }`); `o` `{ today, lang, points }`.

Styles are `assets/css/plan.css`'s (both pages load it), light and dark; the cards around them are the pages' own (`homework.css`, `plan.css` for Pat's link, `day.css` for his). At 390px nothing scrolls the page sideways: a timeline wider than its box scrolls inside it.

### 21.3 The rules

- **Days** are columns counted in whole days from the household's today (`'YYYY-MM-DD'` from the view, never the device's clock or zone), so the clock change of Sun Nov 1 moves nothing.
- **The overview**: one row per open assignment (status `open`), by due date; across 21 days from today, with a header of days (the month on the first day and on each 1st, the weekday's letter and the date; each week from its Monday; today's column marked). A bar from the first step's day (today when it has no plan or its first step is earlier) to the due date, the done share filled; milestones as diamonds (filled when done) on their days when the steps are known, else only `next_milestone`'s; one not done whose day has passed on today's column; a done one before today, or one past the 21 days, isn't drawn. The due date is a flag at the bar's end; past the 21 days the bar runs to the edge with an arrow, and its line says "due Fri Dec 4"; a plan whose first step is past the 21 days has no bar in them, only the arrow and its "due …" line; an open one past its due date is marked late (on today's column, no flag). Each row's name is a link when `o.link` gives one, with a label for screen readers ("Essay: a river's journey · steps from Mon Nov 9 · milestone Outline Tue Nov 10 · milestone Full draft Mon Nov 16 · due Fri Nov 20 · 0 of 7 steps done"), and its line "English · 0 of 7 · due Fri Nov 20".
- **The chart**: days from the first step's day (or today, when earlier) to the due date, at most 63, keeping today and what's ahead: past 63 days it starts at today or 62 days before the end, whichever is earlier, and past 63 days from there an arrow and "due …" under it. A step outside the days is never drawn on another day: past the last one the arrow stands for it; before the first, a done one is left out and one not done sits in the first column, "from earlier" (each phase's count and minutes still count every step). A row per phase, the steps up to and including each milestone (named by the milestone, with a diamond), then "Check and turn in" ("The plan" when there is no milestone), each with its count and minutes ("3 steps · 50 min"); each step a block on its day with its minutes, done ones filled, a milestone's diamond, the turn-in's flag, one not done whose day has passed dashed, "from earlier" (only while the assignment is open: a done or dropped one's unticked steps aren't behind); today's line; days without homework time shaded when the page knows his week. Not drawn for a plan on fewer than 2 distinct days. A chart wider than its box (a phone shows about 15 days) **opens on what's next**: the day before today, or before its first open step when that is later (so a plan starting in three weeks opens on its steps, not on empty days); the rows' names stay in view while it scrolls (beside the days from 540px, over them below), a shade on an edge says more days are that way, and the box scrolls by keyboard (`tabindex="0"`, a region named as the chart).
- **His path**: a dot a day from today to the due date (at most 14, then "… due <day>"), today highlighted, the due date a star, flags on the milestones' days (filled when done; one not done whose day has passed on today's dot; those done before today counted at the start), the days with his open steps marked; one line on top (§21.1). Nothing for a plan not given to him, done, without steps, or past its due date: **his screen never says late**. Calm and short: nothing about other people, no red.

### 21.4 What isn't drawn, and why

- **Points on his path.** The owner's example line ends "+20 points now", and `path()` draws it when the page gives `o.points`. His view doesn't carry what a milestone pays at his tick: `pts_milestone` isn't in it, and a step's `waiting` is the check's half, there only once he has ticked it (§3.4). Rather than guess (an odd value splits unevenly, and the weekly cap can take some), the line leaves the points out; his screen still says "+20 now · +20 more when Pat checks it" the moment he ticks one (§8.1). Adding it would need the view to carry the value: a migration, left for later.
- **Pat's overview starts every bar today and shows only the next milestone.** Pat's list carries `steps` and `steps_done` as counts and `next_milestone`, not the steps' days (§3.4); the assignment's screen has every step, so its chart is complete. Reading every assignment to draw the overview would be a request per assignment, which this release doesn't add.
- **Jordan's paths are for given plans only**: his view has no steps before a plan is given (it says "Being planned"), and a short plan due in under 4 days with no milestone is already all on Tonight and Due soon.
- **No chart** for a plan on one day: a single column says nothing the plan editor doesn't.

### 21.5 Tests

- **e2e** — `plan.test.js`: the three functions (columns, clipping at both ends, past the range, a first step past it, late, the done share, milestones known or only next, phases, behind steps and none on a done or dropped one, a chart past 63 days keeping today with every block on its own day, where a chart opens and `tlScroll` at 390px and in a wide box, the path's folding and its line, a past flag by its date, a lesson's "Do by", every flag done, no plan, a hostile title and class, Spanish, the clock-change week, drawn again in New York, whose clocks go back on Nov 1). `homework.test.js`: Next 3 weeks and the chart on the parents' page (Sam too), no request of its own (the steps in the page's one batched read, none for one assignment), a save moving a block, the essay's chart opening on its due day at 390px, a 38-character class wrapping inside the name column at 1280px, none when nothing is open. `tutor.test.js`: Next 3 weeks inside Assignments (the fixed sections unchanged), every row's bar, fill, diamond, flag, late mark and arrow from Pat's counts, the links, a hostile title as text, the essay's chart above the editor with his week shaded, a save moving a block, the cell model's chart opening on its steps (all three and the due day in view, the names too) at 390px and 1280px, a 38-character class wrapping inside the name column, none for a plan on one day or none, none when nothing is open, Spanish, no new call. `day.test.js`: Your path under Tonight, three then "+2 more" (open through a read), each line ("from Fri Oct 30"), the links, the path at the top of `#plan/<id>` drawn again from reads (to "Every flag done!") and from a Save's and a Re-plan's answer, four flags won before today at 390px with "Today" clear of the next day's letter, none for a plan being planned, none when no plan needs one, no points in any line, Spanish. All three at 390px and 1280px, light and dark, with no sideways page scroll; screenshots to `TIMELINE_SHOTS` when it is set.
- **realdb** — `timeline.test.js`: a two-week essay with two milestones saved and given through the API and its first step ticked through his token, drawn from the real answers on Alex's page, Jordan's My day and Pat's link (made with `save_tutor_link`).
- **docs** — this section; nothing new to check, as no signature or table changes.

### 21.6 Deploy

Merge; the site changes on next load. No owner steps, nothing to paste, nothing to undo but the merge.

## Build notes

Where the build had to choose because this document was silent, ambiguous or couldn't be done as written: the smallest sensible choice, on the safe side. Each is in the code and its tests.

**Database (0005, PR A)**
- `private.ai_input` takes a fifth parameter, `p_from text` (`'tutor'` or `'parent'`): §6.4's `<request from="tutor">` names the asker's role, which the four parameters of §3.5 couldn't carry. `homework_ai_preview` passes `'parent'`, Pat's preview `'tutor'`, and `homework_ai_start` the asker's side. §3.5 is changed in step.
- `private.free_minutes` also returns `first_free time`, the first free minute of the day's homework time, which §7.1's on-time window is built around; §3.5 is changed in step.
- `private.free_minutes` is granted to `service_role` as well as authenticated: §3.6 revokes `homework_week` only from public and anon, so `service_role` keeps Supabase's default grant on it, and `homework_week` runs as its caller.
- An item with a start time and no end (an event or a task with only a time) is busy for one hour, as `agenda` draws an event without an end; the 15 minutes after it apply when that hour ends inside the homework time.
- `private.place_steps` also answers `minutes_left` (the plan's not-done minutes) and `time_left` (the room from the first day to the last work day before this plan), which the views' bars need, and takes two flags on a step, `fixed` (keeps its day: a person's day, a checkpoint in Re-plan) and `done` (never moves). For an assignment due today, or already past its due date, the last work day is today, and a turn-in past its due date goes on today.
- The writers' own keys, merged into the view they answer with, never reuse a view key: `focus_by_token` answers `block` (not `session`, which in his view is the block still open) and `earned: {focus, on_time, capped}`; a check answers `paid` and `used_up` (not `points`, the view's balance); Make it smaller `new_step_id`; every step action `step_id`; plan saves `plan: {assignment_id, plan_rev, steps, fits, over, horizon, minutes_left, time_left, no_time, talk}`; `assignment_by_token` `assignment_id`; `tutor_assignment_by_token` `assignment_id` and `moved` (how many steps a moved due date placed again). `reward_by_token` and `homework_prefs_by_token` answer the view alone.
- A plan save on an old `p_rev` raises P0001 "This plan was changed since you opened it." and carries no plan: the page reads the view again. A save on an assignment that is done or dropped raises P0001 "This assignment is done or dropped. Open it again first.".
- An answer's writer can change it for 15 minutes from the **first** answer, not from the last change, so a run of edits can't keep the window open.
- No constraint's detail ever reaches a link. PostgREST hands the caller Postgres's DETAIL, and for a not-null or check violation that is the whole row ("Failing row contains …": `created_by`, `answer_by` and `qa_log` with a parent's email, Pat's full label), which §3.4 forbids both links. So `private.plan_check` refuses a step sent without its `minutes` key (22023 "A step is 5 to 60 minutes.", as for any other bad minutes), `tutor_assignment_by_token` refuses a title that cleans to nothing on a change too (22023 "An assignment needs a title and a due date."), and every token writer (Jordan's six, the link's three) turns any constraint error it still meets into 22023 "That can't be saved.", with no detail.
- Uncheck (§4.5) is refused, P0001 "Those points are already spent.", when taking the check's points back (since the owner's change, the check's half only) would put his balance below zero (they were traded since): as for a Void (§8.4), and §4.5 and §8.4 are changed in step. Removing the check but keeping the points would let a later check pay twice.
- `save_tutor_link` lets a change keep the link's own end date even when it has passed, so Off, On and a new name or language work on a link that has ended (the page always sends the date it has); the link stays ended, and only a new date (**Extend**, today to today + 400) brings it back. A new link, or a change to another date, keeps §3.5's rule. §3.5 is changed in step.
- **Undo** and **Dismiss** on an AI draft (§6.10) are in 0005, so PR B needs no migration for them and neither is a device-only change that a reload undoes: `homework_ai_draft(p_assignment, p_action)` for a parent (a thirteenth signed-in function), and an `ai` field (`'undo'` or `'dismiss'`) in `tutor_assignment_by_token`'s `p_fields` for Pat's link, rather than a twelfth token function. Both go through `private.ai_draft_act`. §2.1, §3.3, §3.5, §3.6 and §6.10 are changed in step. Nothing calls them before PR B.
- `'clear'` (parents) takes `p_text` `'answer'` or `'note'`; null clears the answer when there is one, else the coach's note.
- `reward_by_token`'s `'cancel'` finds his waiting ask by `p_key` (the ask's id) or, without it, by `p_reward`.
- `decide_reward`'s **Yes** checks the balance less the points held by his other waiting asks (§8.5's `available`, without this ask's own), so a Yes after a Void can't leave his other waiting asks short.
- `give_points` is refused while points are off (42501 "Points are off."), as `award` would write nothing and the parent would see points that never came.
- A due date a parent changes on the Homework page (a direct update of `assignments`) doesn't move steps in the database; the page's Place again does. A due date moved through Pat's link places again, at once, the steps not done that now fall after the last work day, and a turn-in not done that is no longer on the due date, as the Homework page does (`moved`): left on the old day, a turn-in moved later would be in his Tonight a week early (and ticking it closes the assignment), and one left after an earlier due date would make every save of his plan fail ("A step can't be after the due date.").
- Dropped assignments are deleted 180 days after they were **dropped** (`assignments.dropped_at`, set by the tidy trigger as `done_at` is; §3.2 is changed in step), never counted from the due date: Pat's link can change a due date, and one sent with a drop must not make the row old at once (§3.3: the link drops, never deletes; §13.1 item 11: nothing Pat wrote to him is lost). Like `done_at`, no grant writes it.
- `homework_trim` deletes the household's AI runs older than 90 days, test runs (no kid) too, and clears an old draft's `ai_state` and `ai_stop` with it, unless a run is open.
- A block closed as `'expired'` (open more than 24 hours when the next Start came) has `seen_minutes` 0 and earns nothing. Pat's `record` leaves out expired blocks and his own blocks (no step and no assignment), as it shows homework only.
- `homework_week` answers every day in the range, with `window: null` on a day without homework time; `free_minutes` leaves those days out, as §5.3 says.
- `private.scrub` removes every tutor link's label in the household (and its parts), not only the kid's own links, and every person's names, active or not (a former helper is a name the hub knows).
- `private.scrub` goes further than §6.4 first said, each on the safe side, and §6.4 is changed in step: the invisible characters `clean_text` keeps (U+00AD, the soft hyphen a PDF or a web page leaves inside a word; U+034F, U+180E) are taken out first, from the text and from every listed word; a listed phrase's spaces match any run of spaces and line breaks (Live Text keeps the sheet's line breaks, so "North Field" and "School" can be on two lines); a link without `https://` or `www.` is `[link]` when it is a dotted host with a path, or ends in a common top-level domain written in lower case (so "end.Us", "St.Louis", "e.g." and "essay.docx" stay); every word of a person's name after the first that doesn't start with a lower-case letter is `[name]` as written (case matters there, so the Page family's "read page 12" isn't garbled); and a tutor link's label words that name a class, a role, a title or nothing (`math`, `tutor`, `teacher`, `ms`, `the`, `de`, … in English and Spanish, a fixed list in the function) aren't names, so a link called "Math tutor" or "Pat - math tutor" doesn't take "math" out of the subject, the title and the text (the whole label, and its other words, still go).
- Pat's `preview` is the `'revise'` message once the assignment has steps or a draft, else `'plan'`; null while the AI is off or the link can't ask.
- `erase_homework` also deletes his and his links' rows in `homework_use`.
- The database's messages say "Pat" where §9.7 lists them ("Plan this one with Pat.", "That's a checkpoint. Change it with Pat.", "… or ask Pat."): `friendly()` matches them and the page puts in `me.tutor`.

**plan.js (PR A)**
- **plan.js, Practice (§4.6):** its blocks are sent with a day each (one per homework day from the first day to the do-by day, from the week the page has and past it by its weekdays), not without days: placement by room alone could stack several blocks on one day. The database keeps a given day only when it has room (§5.4), so nothing else changes.
- **plan.js, Pat's view (§3.4) has no `length`:** starter minutes are cut to his first length where the page knows it (the Homework page, My day). On Pat's link they are cut when `tutor_view_by_token` answers a `length` key, else they keep their own minutes (at most 25, the longest length). `private.plan_save` cuts a starter step's minutes to his first length when it is saved in any case (§4.4), so only the editor's sum can read longer before the save.
- **plan.js, Spanish dates:** `fmt.rel` gives a Spanish day with its article ("el mié", "el vie 13 nov"), and `t()` turns "de el"/"a el" into "del"/"al", so plan.js's words read "para {day}", "de {day}" rather than §9.7's sample "del {day}" (the output is the same).
- **plan.js, a dead tutor link** says "This link no longer works. Ask the family for a new one." (§9.7 names Jordan): a link that answers null no longer knows whose it was.
- **plan.js, `place()`** is §5.4 for the page's own arithmetic and for tests' fakes, never instead of `private.place_steps`; the turn-in goes on the due date even past the 8-week horizon (it needs no room).
- **plan.js, the end screen's line** reads "+20 · 20 minutes · +10 on time", the form §14 names ("+12 · 12 minutes"), rather than a sample "+20 focus · 20 minutes".
- **plan.js, the day a plan was given:** `given_at` is a timestamp, so the editor finds the household's day from the answer's `as_of` and `server_now` (both views carry them); the Homework page passes its own (else the date is shown as the database wrote it).
- **plan.js, a done step is sent without a day.** The database keeps a done step as it is whatever the list says (§4.4), so its day only meets `plan_check`'s "after the due date": a step ticked early and a due date moved before its day then refused every save of that plan (Save, Give, Place again, a starter plan, the Homework page's due-date re-place), on the Homework page, Pat's link and `#plan/<id>` alike.
- **plan.js, Practice for an assignment** (not a lesson) ends at its last work day (`lastWorkDay`: the day before the due date, two before when that is 7 or more days away), as placement does (§5.4): its last block on the due date made the editor refuse its own starter plan ("Only the turn-in goes on the due date."). A lesson's day picker offers its do-by day, a work day; an assignment's stops the day before.
- **plan.js, `LIMITS.decline` 120:** Not now's word, `decide_reward`'s `p_note` and `reward_asks.note` (a step's note is 160).
- **plan.js, the editor's boxes** show done steps and can't be ticked there: `tutor_step_by_token` has no `'done'`, and Jordan ticks on his own screen. Pat and the parents change a step's kind with a Kind list in ⋯, which covers "milestone on or off" (§9.4).

**Joining the database and plan.js**
- Jordan's view carries `plan_rev` on each assignment (§3.4 is changed in step): `plan_by_token` requires `p_rev`, the `#plan/<id>` editor sends `assignment.plan_rev`, and §3.4 gave his page no way to know it.
- Jordan's `on_time` carries `points`, the parents' on-time value (§3.4 is changed in step), for §9.7's "+{n}" when they changed it from 10; `onTimeLine` reads it and says +10 without it.
- A milestone check past the week's cap answers `used_up: true` (the database's key; §4.5 names none), and plan.js reads that key for "This week's milestone points are used up.". `tutor_step_by_token` leaves out the check's `paid`, the points it paid, since the link never sees points (§3.4, §9.5); the parents' `homework_step` keeps it.
- plan.js's `friendly()` also knows four more of the database's messages a plan save or Pat's link can meet: "That plan isn't on your list.", "That step isn't on this link.", "This assignment is done or dropped. Open it again first." and "That plan is too long.", so a Spanish reader gets them in Spanish.
- It also knows the limits Jordan meets on My day, where trying again can't help: "At most 20 steps." (Make it smaller; matched whole, so another number keeps the database's words), "Three asks are waiting already. Wait for a parent first.", "Five suggestions are waiting already. Wait for a parent first." and "Fifteen of your own are open already.". A Spanish reader was told "Algo salió mal. Inténtalo de nuevo." for each. The Spanish words are to be checked with the family's Spanish speaker, as every Spanish word is (§17.2).
- And two that the database's review fixes added: "Those points are already spent." (Pat's **Uncheck** once the milestone's points were traded, as a Void) and "That can't be saved." (the token writers' last guard, a constraint error said without its detail), for Pat's link and, the second, Jordan's My day. Without them a Spanish reader got the general line.

**The Homework page and Today (PR A)**
- The Homework page also reads `events` (the homework-time events, by the ids in `homework_times`): the kid line names his homework time from them ("Homework time Mon–Thu 4:30–5:15pm, Sun 5:00–5:45pm") and **Change** opens each one in the Calendar's form. §9.1's list of reads didn't name it. It doesn't read `ai_runs` or `household` before PR B (both are for the AI parts).
- It reads `homework_week` for 56 days from today, not the default 14: the bars' "Homework time left" and the editor's day picker need every homework day up to the 8-week horizon. The strip still shows 7 days.
- **Erase** says what it deletes and that the homework-time events stay; it says nothing about Anthropic's copy before PR B, since nothing on any page mentions the AI before it (§2.4).
- Pat's link is sent with the link on its own line at the end ("Hi Pat, here is Jordan's homework page. It shows only his homework. Please open it on your own phone or computer:" then the link), not mid-sentence with a full stop after it, which some messaging apps take as part of the link.
- **Extend 180 days** sets the end to 180 days after the later of its end date and today, at most today + 400 (`save_tutor_link`'s limit).
- A due date changed on the Homework page saves only the fields that changed, then places again (`save_homework_plan`, those steps without a day) the steps not done that now fall after the last work day, and a turn-in that is no longer on the due date, and says "3 steps moved: due date changed" (§4.1). The database moves nothing on a direct update (build note above).
- "Edit rewards" is ⋯ on each reward (its name, points, on or off, Delete) and **+ Reward**; the list is in `sort_order`, a new one last. There is no reordering control.
- Homework time can't be removed from the Homework page: **Change** opens the event, where the Calendar can delete it (which removes the link, as §5.1 says).
- The kid picker lists the kids with a `homework_kids` row, on or off; any other active kid gets "Turn on homework for <name>" on the switches card.
- Questions and notes names a coach's note's writer from the step's `checked_by` (a note given with Looks done) or `updated_by` (one given with Not yet), since the step keeps no author for its note; earlier notes in `qa_log` carry only the role ("A parent's note", "Pat's note"). A cleared answer opens the question again, so it is listed with **Answer** as well as "(cleared)".
- **Copy to next week** copies the lesson and its steps (not given, without days, so the database places them), and the page stays on the lesson copied.
- Today's homework request for steps reads those touched in the last 30 days (`updated_at`), as the Homework page shows 30 days of assignments, and it reads all of the asking kid's ledger rows, not only this week's: his balance needs them.
- Today's lines sit under the person chips (after tonight's dinner) and are the same under every chip; **Not now** there is one tap, with no word (the word is on the Homework page).
- Today and the Homework page read a kid's points ledger a page at a time (`.range()`, 1,000 rows each, oldest first by `at` and `id`, until a page comes back empty or brings nothing new): the API answers at most its **Max rows** (1,000 on Supabase unless changed) and cuts the rest without an error, and ledger rows are never trimmed (§13.3), so after about two school years one read cut his balance short, and Today's (unordered) read could lose this week's rows too. `mock.js` and the real-database stand-in cut a read at 1,000 rows, as Supabase does.
- A due date (or kind) changed on the Homework page places again from the plan **as it is now**, not the page's copy: its `plan_rev` is read first, then its steps, and that `plan_rev` is sent, so a step Jordan or Pat added, moved or changed since the page read the plan is kept (§4.4's `p_rev`). A change landing between the two reads makes the save refused, and it is read again once; never undone.
- **Yes** and **Not now** (Today and the Homework page) say what `decide_reward` answered: an ask the other parent already answered, or that he cancelled, comes back as it is with no error, so the toast says "Already answered Not now: …", "Already approved: …" or "Jordan cancelled that ask: …", not what was tapped. On Today both of an ask's buttons wait for the answer.
- **Not now**'s word on the Homework page takes at most 120 characters, `decide_reward`'s limit.
- The read on focus is held back while anything on the Homework page is being typed: the homework-time, rewards, points-values and link forms (and the weekday chips) too, not only a note, a price, the assignment's fields and the editor.

**My day and the tutor link (PR A)**
- `day.html` isn't changed by this part: the page part adds `plan.js` (after `shoplist.js`) and `plan.css` (after `day.css`) with the release's `?v=`. `day.js` works without `plan.js` (no card, no homework request, the day as before), which `day.test.js` checks by blocking `plan.js` (a phone that kept an older `day.html`, or a failed fetch).
- `homework_by_token` answering null while the My day link still works (homework turned off a moment ago): the page reads `get_person_day` again and shows the day with "Homework isn't on your My day right now.", as v1.2 does for the list; only a link that no longer works says so (§3.5 reads every null as "this link no longer works").
- His view carries who wrote a coach's note (`coach.by`, a role), but not who answered a question, so an answer reads "Answer: …" under the step (never a name that might be wrong); notes read "Pat: …" or "Your parents: …".
- **+ Homework** sends `p_minutes` (so `'add'` does Quick too) only when the due date is within 3 days: `assignment_by_token` refuses the whole add with minutes for a later one ("Plan this one with Pat."). With no homework time at all, though, an `'add'` with minutes keeps his homework, with no plan (his view says `no_time`), instead of refusing the whole add with "Set a homework time first.", a message for a parent: writing his homework down is the habit §4.1 is for, and his phone can't tell before it asks. `'quick'` on its own still gets that 22023 (§5.4), and works once a parent sets a homework time. The database test that expected the whole add refused ("… and so does Quick") now checks both: the add kept with no step, and Quick refused. A later one is added with no plan and shows under **Due soon** with "No plan yet · Plan this one with Pat.".
- **Quick** takes his guess from chips (10, 15, 20, 30, 45), one tap each; the step it makes is **Now**, as is the one **+ Homework** makes.
- **Stop** and **End the block** end the block at once, since the server counts the minutes to its answer; "What did you get done? (optional)" is asked when the ring runs out, or when he comes back to a block that ended, with **Step done** and **More to do**.
- The card's **Start** uses the length remembered on the device when there is one (§7.2), else his first length, and "alone".
- "On time · +10 once 5 minutes are done" shows on `#focus` after his own Start (the start's `block.on_time`), and after a reload from the view's `session.on_time` (added with the owner's change below; before it, a reload lost the line).
- The card says "Your block is over. How did it go? ›" for a block that has ended: `get_person_day`'s card has `ends_in_s` but no clock time. `#homework` and `#focus` say "Your 20-minute block ended at 4:52. How did it go?" (the time from the view's `as_of` and `server_now`).
- A Start while a block is open goes to that block's screen without a call (the view's `session` says it is open); the database's "Finish the block you started first." does the same.
- A milestone his plan can't reach shows under its assignment in **Due soon** (from `behind.milestone`), as "Flashcards made was planned for Fri Oct 30.
