Pick a new day with Pat ›", with the date rather than a weekday; it opens **Ask Pat** on that milestone with the line filled in. Re-plan answers "Planned again from today" or "Doesn't fit…".
- **Due soon** lists every assignment not in Tonight's no-plan rows: given plans with their two bars, plans being made, ones with no plan due 4 or more days out, and done ones of the last 3 days.
- The screen wake lock is asked for again whenever `#focus` is drawn with a block running (after a reload), and on coming back into view (`focus`, `visibilitychange`) on any view while the ring runs, not only inside the Start tap; refused or missing, the tip shows.
- The wake lock follows the block, not the screen (§7.2's "released at the end"): kept while the ring runs on any view (`#homework`, the day), and taken again on coming back to any of them (the browser lets go of it while the page is hidden), let go of when the ring runs out (a timer, whatever is on screen) and when a Start gets no block (no answer, a 503, a refusal); never asked for again for an end screen whose ring already ran out, and never two at once. Before, a block that ended away from `#focus` kept the screen on, and one still running on `#homework` or the day lost the lock for good once the page had been hidden.
- **Chime even on silent** sets `'playback'` from 3 seconds before the chime until its last tone has stopped (or the chime is called off), not from Start to the end: §7.2's "just for the chime". Before, the end of the block set `'auto'` again while the chime was still sounding, which a silenced iPhone mutes.
- The chime is set again on the audio clock whenever it no longer falls on the ring's end: coming back into view, on any view (`#focus`, `#homework`, the day), resumes the `AudioContext` the Start tap made (iOS stops a hidden page's audio clock, so the tones would come that much late); tones still more than 2 seconds away when the ring runs out are called off, on any view (a timer at the ring's end, and on coming back); and after a reload the `AudioContext` is made at his first tap on `#focus` (iOS needs a tap), never for a block he has just ended. §7.2's "scheduled on the audio clock at Start" stands: this sets it again.
- A second tap on **Stop**, **End the block**, **Done** on a block with no step, or the end screen's buttons while the End is on its way changes nothing: the buttons are disabled at the first tap, and that End's answer is drawn. Before, the second tap replaced the End waiting, the first answer was dropped and the screen froze until a focus, `online` or `visibilitychange`.
- **I'm stuck** on a block started on an assignment with no plan (**Start** on a Tonight row) has no step: **Make it smaller** and **Ask Pat** first make it a one-step plan with **Quick** (the block's length, given at once, as his Quick does) and move the block to that step (`'switch'`), then act on it, so Step done still finishes the assignment. A block on neither a step nor an assignment shows neither button. Before, both always failed with "That step isn't on your plan."
- **Make it smaller** keeps one key (the new step's id) per sheet until an answer, and a sheet sends once at a time: a second tap, or a tap after "Can't reach Home Hub" when the first one landed, finds the step it made (one step, never two; §4.5).
- day.js's own Spanish words turn "a el"/"de el" into "al"/"del" before a `{day}` as plan.js's `t()` does: "Pasado al mié", not "Pasado a el mié".
- The tutor link's page takes its title ("Homework · Jordan") and language from each answer's `kid` and `language`; it keeps nothing on the device.

**Docs (PR A)**
- The label-word check (§13.1 item 8, §15) is `tests/docs/labels.test.js`, in the docs suite. It reads every text file under `supabase/` (so PR B's prompt in `hub-jobs` too), `assets/` and `tests/` (every test, not only the fixtures), and the pages and docs, which §13.1 item 8 names ("no … doc describes a condition") though §15's list leaves them out; `tests/docs/` itself, which holds the list, is the one folder left out. Words are matched whole, case aside; words with an everyday meaning in this code are left off the list ("add", "odd", "sped", "disabled", and "therapy", the title of a private appointment in `calendar.test.sql`). It also checks its own matcher against each listed word and that the scan reached the files that matter, so it can't pass by reading nothing.
- The docs check's new counts: §2.1's tables (by name), `household` columns and public and private functions against 0005, and §3.6's "nineteen" against the grants (number words up to "fifty-nine", so "twenty-five" reads). It also checks that `CLAUDE.md` names the scripts `index.html` and `day.html` load, in order, and `day.html`'s stylesheets; that every `?x=` `day.js` reads (`t`, `c`, and `h` from v1.3) is described in `CLAUDE.md` and v2-design §4; and that `tests/README.md` names every browser and real-database test file. Each is a list the code makes, so a later script, mode or test file that isn't written up fails it.
- The README's Homework section has no AI part (§15 marks it PR B), as no page mentions the AI before PR B (§2.4); its off-lines are §13.5's three, `homework_ai` included, since 0005 adds that column.

**Joining the Homework page, My day and the docs (PR A)**
- plan.js's `check()` now takes the assignment's `kind` and follows the database's due-date rules: "Only the turn-in goes on the due date." is for an assignment due after today and for a step new or moved there (`plan_save`), never for a lesson (its do-by day is its last work day, §5.4), and "after the due date" counts from the later of the due date and today (`plan_check`). Before, the editor refused a lesson's Practice plan whose last block fell on a do-by day with homework time, on the Homework page, Pat's link and `#plan/<id>` alike, and a plan for an assignment past its due date.
- With `day.html` loading `plan.js` and `plan.css`, the tests no longer add them (`mock.js`'s `dayWithPlan` is gone); `day.test.js` checks the day without `plan.js` by blocking its request.

**Real-database tests (PR A)**
- The homework flows of §14's `flows.test.js` row are in a file of their own, `tests/realdb/homework.test.js`, on its own copy of the full template: they move the household's clock from Tue Oct 27 to Mon Nov 2 and Wed Nov 4 (homework time, on-time starts and points depend on the day and the hour), which `flows.test.js`'s flows, pinned to Oct 27, can't share. `flows.test.js` keeps the rest of that row: `MY_DAY_FNS` gains the eleven, and its schema check reads 5 (it read 4: the full template has every migration, so that number moves with each release).
- Alex sets three rewards, not one: the first visit's form asks for three (§8.5), the first instant, and one more with **+ Reward** for the ask. Besides the 20-minute block that pays +20 and +10 on time the moment its ring runs out, a second block whose ring the phone runs to the end while the server sees 7 minutes pays +7: the points follow the server's clock, not the device's.
- `v4db.test.js` also pastes 0005 into the running hub (as `jobs.test.js` pastes 0004 on `jobs3`): every `get_person_day`, every `day_text` and the morning email are the same before and after, the realdb side of the database suite's homework-off check (5d). No job reads homework data in PR A (`hub-jobs` is unchanged), so `jobs.test.js` is unchanged; `homework.test.js` checks that Jordan's text and the morning email carry his homework-time event and nothing of his plan, points or rewards.
- "Pat's link reads no family table" is checked on the requests the page makes (only the four tutor functions, signed out) and on the page's text; `access.test.js` checks the answers themselves and passes Pat's token to every other anonymous function. Its `TOKEN_FNS` list, which no check used, is now compared with the catalog: anon may run exactly those twenty functions.
- `HOMEWORK_TABLES`, `HOMEWORK_FNS` and `HOMEWORK_CALLS` live in `tests/realdb/lib.js`, so the four version files (`v1db` to `v4db`) forbid one list.

**Joining the real-database tests (PR A)**
- `tests/e2e/homework.test.js` and `tests/realdb/homework.test.js` share a name, so the docs check's "`tests/README.md` names every browser and real-database test file" now looks for each file in its own suite's section of `tests/README.md` (before, one write-up anywhere in the file passed for both). `tests/run.sh`'s header gives the real-database suite's new time, about 3 minutes, as `CLAUDE.md` and `tests/README.md` do.

**The owner's changes: immediate gratification (PR A, before 0005 was applied anywhere)**

The owner approved six earn-only changes after the first build, with no penalties and the server's clock as the only authority: the payout the second a block ends, with a small celebration and a live count while it runs; a next-reward bar; instant rewards; milestones paid half at the tick and half at the check; 10-minute blocks offered first; and bigger numbers (§0, §1.2 decisions 2, 3, 11 and 15, §7, §8). 0005 was changed in place, as it had been applied nowhere. Where the build had to choose:
- **The payout is an End.** A new `focus_sessions.result`, `'time'`: the phone sends End `'time'` when the ring runs out (a timer at the end, on `#focus`, `#homework` and the day; the card, which has no session, reads his view when its ring runs out), or at once when a page sees a block whose end has passed. It pays as any End does; his answer then comes as a second End that replaces `'time'` (`done`, `more`, `stopped`), does the step's part, keeps a note he typed (or the one there), and pays nothing more. A tap on **Step done** or **More to do** while the `'time'` End is on its way waits for it and is sent after it. **Stop**, **End the block** and **Done** on a block with no step are still one End, as before. A second `'time'` answers the first.
- **The live count is the server's rule run forward:** `session.earn` (per minute, the on-time points when the block is on time, and the day's cap left on the block's start day) and `plan.estimate(earn, seconds, minutes)`, from the server's `started_at` by the page's anchored clock, redrawn once a minute. It stops at the block's length and at the cap's room, so it never shows more than the End pays. A block of his own (no step, no assignment), points off, or a block over 24 hours old has `earn: null` and no count.
- **The celebration** plays on the view he is on when the points come (the end screen, `#points`, `#homework` after a milestone's tick, or the day's card when a block's ring runs out there), and only then: a view drawn without it (the day with no card, his plan) lets it go, so it never plays later, out of context. Its "+N" is what was paid (the End's `earned.focus + earned.on_time`, a tick's `paid`), counted up to the balance the answer carries, whatever else moved the balance during the block (a parent's Give, a Yes on Today); his answer after a `'time'` End pays nothing and celebrates nothing. The balance counts up from the old one in at most 20 steps of 40 ms, a "+N" appears beside it (`.hw-plus`, its pop off with reduced motion, and the count shown at once), and two short triangle tones (784 and 1047 Hz) play on the `AudioContext` made in a tap, only when there is one: no sound on a page that never had a tap. The chime's sine tones are unchanged.
- **The next-reward bar** is the server's (`goal` in his view and on the card): `next`, the cheapest active, priced reward costing more than his free points (balance less what waiting asks hold) and with no waiting ask of his (its points are held already: the bar never says he still needs points for it), with `need`; `now`, the dearest one he can cash or ask for now. `plan.goalBar` draws it; on `#focus` it adds the live count, so the bar moves while he works and says "<reward> when this block ends!" once the count reaches it.
- **Instant rewards are asks decided at once**, not a new table: `reward_asks.status` `'cashed'` (asked and decided at `private.now()`, by him) and the `'trade'` entry of −cost with its `ask_id`, so Today, the Homework page, the decided list and Erase see them as any trade. The day's limit counts his `'cashed'` asks decided on the household's day. His view's `rewards[]` gains `instant` and `can_cash`, and `points.cash_left`. An instant reward can still be asked for when today's are used up. Today reads the cashed asks of the last day and keeps those of today (the household's day, as the page shows it).
- **The milestone's two halves are two ledger kinds:** `'milestone_done'` (his tick: `(pts_milestone + 1) / 2`, so an odd value pays the larger half first; unique per step) and `'milestone'` (the check: the rest). The weekly cap counts both. His own Undo within 15 minutes deletes the `'milestone_done'` entry that tick paid (its `at` is the tick's `done_at`, both `private.now()`), the ledger's second kind of delete, so a mis-tap is put right and a new tick pays again; it is refused when that would take his balance below zero. An entry an earlier tick paid (kept by **Not yet** or a parent's Undo of his tick, which wasn't his mistake) stays, so his Undo of a later tick takes nothing and is never refused; and a half a parent voided stays with its void (they cancel): no take-back ever deletes a `'void'`. A check of a milestone nobody ticked pays both, and its uncheck takes both and the tick (`done_at = checked_at` and `done_by = checked_by` say the check ticked it). A check's `used_up` compares what it paid with what it had to pay: its half unless a `'milestone'` entry is there already, and the first half only when nobody ticked it and no entry is there. `void_points` takes either kind. `step_by_token`'s `'done'` answers `paid`.
- **10 minutes first** is `homework_kids.focus_minutes`' default; starter plans are cut to it when saved (an essay's first steps become 10 minutes each), as before with 20. The real-database flow has Alex choose 20 on the switches card first, so its plan is placed as before.
- **"Under 5 minutes"** became "Under a minute: no points this time." (`under_minute`), as a minute now pays.
- The changed expectations in the db, e2e and realdb tests are the owner's new rules (the numbers, the halves, the payout at the ring's end, the default length); none was weakened, and each new behaviour has its own cases.

**Review of the immediate-reward changes (PR A, before 0005 was applied anywhere)**
- **What a take-back takes.** His own Undo takes back only the half that tick paid, and an uncheck only what the check paid (its half; and, when the check ticked a milestone nobody had, the tick and that first half too, opening the assignment again if the check closed it). A parent's Void is never deleted by anyone's take-back: the entry and its void stay and cancel, so a tick or check again pays nothing and the correction's reason stays. Before, his Undo deleted the half and its void together (a re-tick then paid the 20 again), took back the half Not yet had kept when he undid a re-tick that paid nothing (or was refused for it), and an uncheck left a check's own tick and first half for good.
- **"Used up" only when the cap took something.** A check after Not yet, or after a parent's Undo, wanted the whole 40 while the first half was already paid, and said "This week's milestone points are used up." with the cap far away.
- **A Yes waits for the other writers.** `decide_reward` takes his `homework_kids` row's lock (before the ask's, as `reward_by_token` does) and then reads the balance, as `void_points`, `reward_by_token`, his Undo and an uncheck do. Before, a Yes that read the balance while his Undo was taking 20 back wrote its trade after the Undo, below zero. `homework.test.sql` checks it with a second session (`dblink`): a Yes waits while a Void holds his row, then refuses.
- **The bar never names an asked reward** (`goal.next` leaves out rewards with a waiting ask); with nothing else above his free points, `next` is null.
- **The price his button showed** (`reward_by_token`'s new `p_cost`, required for `'cash'`): a parent's price change between his screen and his Yes is refused, 22023 "That reward's price changed." (in his words: "That reward's price changed. Look again before you get it."), and the screen reads the reward again.
- **The confirm tap is never the second tap of a double tap.** "Yes, get it · −15" is drawn where "Get it now" was, so it is drawn disabled and takes taps 400 ms later. A tap test (`page.mouse.dblclick`, two `touchscreen.tap`s) sends nothing.
- **A cash-in's key lives until its answer** (`cashKeys`, per reward): Cancel, Get it now again and other views keep it; an answer, a refusal, or a read whose `asks` hold it (it landed: "Got it", no Yes left) end it. A refused or unanswered cash-in, and a refused ask, read his view again, so `can_cash`, `cash_left` and the bar are the server's.
- **A reload between the paid End and his answer** used to lose "How did it go?" (the block is no longer open, and the answer lived only in memory): his view's `answer_due` (and the card's `answer_due: true`) carry his latest block when its result is still `'time'` (within 24 hours of its start), as the End answered it, and `day.js` rebuilds the end screen from it, so `#focus`, `#homework`'s line and the card ask again and his answer ticks the step.
- **The bar on the whole timer screen:** the end screen, after his answer and the break draw `plan.goalBar` too (the live count's extra only while the ring runs).
- **A toast check made exact** (`day.test.js`, a lost cash-in tapped again). It read the newest toast as soon as any toast showed. The lost answer's "Can't reach Home Hub" stays 6 seconds, and the fake marks a call done before its answer reaches the page, so about one run in four it read that toast before "Got it" arrived. The lost answer's toast is now checked, then cleared, as the file's other lost-answer cases do; the "Got it" check is unchanged.

**The school feed, server side (0006)**
- **Assignments from school are made on a tap, not on every read** (§11.3, §11.4): the feed fills `school_items`; an item becomes an assignment (`added_by` `'school'`) only on Jordan's **Quick** or **Plan with Pat**, or a parent's **Add**. Making every school item an assignment at once would fill his Due soon, Pat's link and the AI's caps with every class's work; New from school is the kid-first draft's inbox. After that, the school's changes reach the assignment a field at a time (`applied` holds what the feed last wrote), never over a person's change.
- **The description is kept** as the item's `details`, and becomes the assignment's teacher's text (P7 in §19 had chosen not to keep it; the owner's brief for this build asks for "the description if present"). It goes only where pasted text goes (never Jordan's view, never a log), is cleaned by `private.clean_block` and cut to 4,000 characters, and is emptied 30 days after the due date like pasted text.
- **Members may read the status card and `school_feeds`** (§12 had "admins select"): the rows hold no address, only its host and last 4 characters. Every change is the parents'. The page itself shows School links to the parents only, as it does the tutor links (below).
- **`school_store` takes a fifth parameter, `p_repeats int default 0`**: the read's count of `RRULE`/`RDATE` events, which §11.3's first-import check shows (`last_repeats`); §12's four parameters couldn't carry it.
- **The parents' actions on an item** are a fifth signed-in function, `school_item_action(p_item, p_action)` (`'add'`, `'hide'`, `'show'`), which §12 didn't name ("a parent can bring it back", "a parent made it an assignment"); the status card is `school_feed_status(p_kid)`, so the page needs one read for the links, the first-import check, the hidden and new items and the moved and gone assignments.
- **Jordan's actions are `'quick'`, `'with_tutor'` and `'hide'`**; `'plan'` ("I'll draft it myself") waits for §12.5. Quick goes through `assignment_by_token`'s own `'quick'`, so its rules (due within 3 days, "Plan this one with Pat.", "Set a homework time first.", the resend) are v1.3's exactly, and a refusal undoes the assignment made for it.
- **The first-import check applies to the Assignments link only**; Schedule and Games/Practices items are busy time at once (an all-day item never is).
- **Missing is counted by key, not by time**: an item is missing from a read when its key isn't among the read's keys (two reads in the same second, as in the tests, still count).
- **A link is due when `next_at` has passed**: a new or replaced link gets `next_at` = now; a good read sets it 50 minutes on and a failure 1, 2, 4, then 8 hours on, each 10 minutes early, so the hourly run at minute 23 finds it due. **Stale** is a link due for over 2 hours and not read: the job isn't running.
- **The lease is 5 minutes** (`private.school_sync`); a store after it answers `late` and changes nothing, and the run stops reading.
- **His view and Pat's are wrapped**, as 0004 wrapped `digest_for`: 0005's bodies are renamed `_v5` and unchanged, so the 0005 tests' exact key lists stay green with no school link, and `from_school` and `school` are added only when there is something to say. `free_minutes` is replaced with 0005's body plus marked lines; `homework_card` was too, until the join below.
- **`school.ts` keeps every fetch failure to a fixed code** and never writes to the console; `tests/fn/school.test.ts` greps the source for `console.` and `message(`. A body read stops at 3 MB or when the 15 seconds are up, whichever comes first.
- Not built with the school feed (§12.6): the homework line in `day_text`, Jordan's own drafts, pace in placement. 0006 changes no email, text or wall event (step 5d with school links stored proves it).

**The school feed's pages (0006)**
- The two sides were built side by side and joined on the database's names and shapes (§12.8): the pages call `school_feed_status`, `set_school_feed` (Remove is `p_url` null), `check_school_feed`, `pause_school_feed`, `school_item_action`, `school_item_by_token`, and hub-jobs' `school` action for **Check now**; `mock.js`'s fakes (`withSchool`, `schoolRpc`, `school_item_by_token`) follow the migration, and `tests/realdb/school.test.js` runs the same flows on the real 0006.
- **School links** (Homework → Switches, parents on schema 6; Sam gets no row and no request, as for the tutor links, though the database would let him read the status): each link's status is "Not added", "Added · first read within the hour", "Added · not read yet" or "Last read today 10:23am · 23 items" (with "Paused · " before it when paused), and under it the host and last 4 characters ("academy.school.example …a1b2"), the last read's stop in words (any code it doesn't know: "The last read didn't work. Home Hub tries again within the hour."), the stale line ("Not read for over 3 hours: the hourly read may have stopped. Run supabase/cron.sql again (see the README).") and how many repeating events were read once. Buttons: **Add link**, **Replace**, **Pause** / **Resume**, **Remove** (asks first), and **Check now** once a link is there (its answer in words: "Checked 1 link · 3 items", "Nothing to read: each link was read in the last minute.", or, while the hourly read holds the lease, "The hourly read is running right now. Try again in a minute.").
- A pasted link is checked on the page first (trimmed; `webcal://` sent as `https://`; https only; at most 500 characters); the school's host, the feed's path and the `z` value are the database's check, whose refusal the form shows in its own words, with the box still open. Once saved the box is emptied: the page keeps no copy, no answer carries one, and nothing is stored on the device or written to the console (the e2e test looks in the page, its boxes, its address, its storage, the toasts and the console).
- **Check the dates** (§11.2 step 7) shows under the links while the Assignments link isn't checked: before its first read, a line saying the next 5 items will show there; then those items ("History · Read chapter 5 · due Fri Nov 6") with **Looks right** (`'end'`, 0), **Use the first date** (`'start'`, 0) and **One day off**, which opens "Blackbaud shows each one:" **A day earlier** (`'end'`, -1) and **A day later** (`'end'`, 1). Until one is tapped, nothing from the link reaches Jordan or Pat.
- Under it, folded: "New from school for Jordan (n)" (each with **Add to homework** and **Hide**) and "Hidden from Jordan's list (n)" (each with **Show**); and, open, a line for each planned assignment whose date the school moved ("…: the school moved its due date from Fri Nov 20 to Mon Nov 23. Open it to place it again ›") and each one no longer on the school site, each opening its assignment. The folds stay as they were across the redraw after each write.
- An assignment made from school says "from school" in the list and on its page, with "the school moved it to Mon Nov 23" or "no longer on the school site" from the status card's `moved` and `gone`.
- **Jordan's New from school** (`from_school`, ids the school items') comes after Due soon: five, the rest folded ("3 more"), each with §11.4's buttons in one tap: **Quick · 10 min** (his first length, 10 unless a parent changed it; only for one with `quick: true`, due within 3 days, so the database's "Plan this one with Pat." isn't met from this list), **Plan with Pat** ("Plan with your parents" with no tutor link) and **Hide**. Quick's toast names the day the database placed the step ("Planned for tomorrow: Lab report"); Plan with Pat's says it is on his homework to plan with Pat (it then shows under Due soon with "No plan yet"); Hide's says a parent can bring it back. The buttons of an item are off while one tap is on its way; a refusal is said and the item stays ("That isn't on your list from school any more.", "That one is on your homework already.", in Spanish too). "New" for an item that came in the last day isn't shown: the view has no field for it.
- His Due soon says "No longer on the school site" or "The school moved it to Mon Nov 16" on an assignment made from school (its `school` marks); Pat's link says "from school" and the same marks, in the list and on the assignment's page (whose answer has the marks but no `added_by`).
- When the card (`get_person_day`'s `homework`) carries a `from_school` count, it shows "New from school: 2 ›", which opens `#homework` at the list.
- `?v=` is moved to a new value with these files (§15's rule: a release that changes them), so phones don't mix the School links pages with an older `hub.js` or `day.js`.

**The school feed, after its review (0006, edited in place: it is applied nowhere yet)**
- **The item's copy of the teacher's text is trimmed too** (§11.3, §13.3): `private.school_trim`, from `school_begin`, so every hourly run and every Check now, for the whole household. The trim is by the item's due date, the threshold `homework_trim` uses for the assignment's copy, so `school_apply` finds the two equal and never writes the text back. It isn't in `homework_trim` (Jordan's and Pat's writers): that keeps the token writers off the school rows that a read is updating, and the hourly run reaches paused links and kids with homework off, which no writer does. The same step lets past items nobody made an assignment go on every link, as a read does on its own.
- **A run that dies partway**: `school_begin` notes the links it hands over, in order (`private.school_sync.handed`), each `school_store` takes its link off, and `school_finish` clears the rest (links skipped for time stay due and count nothing). A lease that ran out with links still noted means the run died on the first of them: the next `school_begin` makes that one a failed read (`error`, backed off). The ones after it were never tried and stay as they were. Links with failures in a row are handed over after the ones reading well.
- **`school_store` refusing a link's items** (PostgREST turns a body it can't take into an error before the function runs): `school.ts` sends that link's read again with `p_stop` `'error'` and no items. Only if that fails too does the run answer 500.
- **The reader's text cleaning** (`ical.ts`): tags are only what is shaped like one (a known HTML element's name or a namespaced one, then up to the next `>` with no `<` on the way; or `<!…>`, `<?…>`), so a title's "x < 3 and y > 1" stays; a raw `SUMMARY` over 2,000 characters and a raw `DESCRIPTION` over 64,000 are cut before cleaning (about 16 times what is kept), a tag cut in two at the end dropped. NUL, the other control characters but tab and line breaks, DEL and lone surrogates are taken out at the start; `cutText` cuts by code points, as Postgres's `char_length` counts. The calendar test is "a line that starts `BEGIN:VCALENDAR`" written without the `m` flag (`(?:^|[\r\n])[ \t]*BEGIN:VCALENDAR`), the same answer in linear time.
- **Folds are joined on the bytes** (`unfoldBytes`, called by `school.ts`'s body reader before it decodes): CRLF, LF or CR, then one space or tab, go. `parseIcal` still unfolds, for text handed to it directly (the tests), where it is then a no-op.
- **The key without a UID has the class** (`'title:'`, the class, a line break, the title, `'|'` and the first day): `clean_text` leaves no line break in either, so no two class and title pairs give one key. The moved-item step also matches the class, and takes only an item the previous good read saw (`last_seen_at >= last_ok_at`), not gone, due inside the window.
- **Remove keeps the row while items made assignments remain** (`school_feeds.removed_at`): the address and the other items go; the row is not read (`school_begin`), not on the status card (nor its items in check, new or hidden), refuses check and pause ("That school link isn't there any more."), and a store for it answers `removed`. Its first-import answer is taken back (`checked` false), so adding the link again (the same row, through `set_school_feed`'s `on conflict`) asks the check again, while the date rule stays, so the kept items' dates don't churn before it is answered. The assignments' school marks stay. With nothing made from it, Remove deletes the row as before; `school_trim` deletes a removed row once its last item is gone.
- **Resume and homework back on**: `pause_school_feed(…, false)` sets `next_at` to now unless a backoff has it later; turning homework back on does the same through a trigger (`homework_kids_school_due`, after an update of `active` from off to on; its function is SECURITY DEFINER, since a parent may turn homework on with a plain update and `school_feeds` takes no writes). `stale` also needs the kid's homework on and the kid active: a link that isn't read on purpose isn't the job stopping.

**Joining the school feed and the immediate rewards (0005 and 0006 edited in place: applied nowhere yet)**
- The school feed was built on 0005 before the owner's immediate-reward changes. Merged with them, 0006's card, then a copy of 0005's card body with one line added, dropped the two keys those changes gave the card (`answer_due`, a paid block's answer still to come, and `goal`, the next-reward bar): on schema 6 the card had no bar, and a reload on the day lost "How did it go?". 0005's own card checks in `homework.test.sql` failed on the merged database. 0006 now wraps the card as it wraps his view and Pat's (0005's body renamed `private.homework_card_v5`, unchanged; the wrapper adds `from_school`), so whatever 0005's card says, schema 6's says too. `tests/db/run.sh` step 5e holds it: the same household at 0005 and at 0006 with homework on and no school link (a given plan with a milestone, three rewards with one instant, Pat's link; a block paid when its ring ran out and not yet answered, an instant reward cashed, an ask waiting) gives the same `get_person_day` for everyone, `homework_by_token` and `tutor_view_by_token` (the list, the assignment, its AI part) at three times, nothing stripped. `free_minutes` is still replaced with a copy (0005's body plus marked lines); step 5e covers it as well.
- The pages needed nothing beyond the merge: the card shows New from school's line, then the next-reward bar; `#homework`'s redraw key holds both sides' state (New from school's fold and taps in flight, the timer's answer and the celebration); `mock.js`'s view is 0005's (with the rewards' `can_cash`, `goal` and `answer_due`) inside `tkWithSchool`, and its card carries `answer_due`, `goal` and `from_school`.
- **Quick from school is his first length**, which the owner's changes made 10 by default (it was 20): `tests/realdb/school.test.js` expected "Quick · 20 min" and a 20-minute step on a kid nobody had set a length for. It now reads his `focus_minutes`, checks it is the default 10, and expects that on the button and the step. (The browser tests' made-up kid has 20 set, so theirs stay as they were.)
- `?v=` is `20261031c` for the school release: `main` went live with Homework at `20261031b`, and this release changes `day.js` and the Homework page, so phones must fetch them again.

**The AI planner (PR B, no migration)**
- **`homework.ts`** holds the action, with the function's own `fetch` and no SDK (as `google.ts`). The request is §6.5's exactly (`buildRequest`), the prompt and the schema §6.6 and §6.7's text byte for byte (`tests/fn/homework-ai.test.ts` reads both out of this document). `index.ts` hands `deps.background` to `EdgeRuntime.waitUntil`, and on `beforeunload` ends every run still open as `timeout` (`cutOff`); whatever that misses, the lease runs out and `private.ai_sweep` closes it. Without `deps.background` (the tests) the work is awaited before the 202. The cost estimate sums `usage.iterations` at each answering model's own price (a fallback names its own model); the prices are a table in `homework.ts`. A 429 or 529 is tried once more after its `retry-after` (1 to 20 seconds, 2 without one) while the 300-second budget allows; a 429 whose `error_code` is `enforced_spend_limit_reached`, and a 400 that says the workspace's usage limit is reached, are `spend_limit`; a 402, or a 400 about the credit balance, `billing`.
- **Only fixed codes are logged**: one line a run (`homework_ai: <stop or ready> ms=N in=N out=N`) and `homework_ai: finish failed` after two failed `homework_ai_finish` calls. `calendar.test.ts`'s "nothing on the console" check now leaves `homework.ts` out (its pinned file list gains it, §2.2); `homework-ai.test.ts` checks that no teacher's text, key or error body reaches the console.
- **A refusal's category** has no column (and PR B has no migration): the stop is `refusal_<category>` when Anthropic gives one (`ai_runs.stop` takes `^[a-z_]{1,40}$`), else `refusal`. The pages read any `refusal…` as a refusal ("The AI couldn't help with this one."), and the switches card names the category ("The AI declined the last one: cyber").
- **What the SQL checks, and how the answer meets it** (gaps found while building, worked around without a migration):
  - `private.plan_check`'s AI rule wants a "done looks like" on every draft step, a person's locked step too, which may have none: `checkPlan` gives such a step the AI's line for it, or, when the answer left the step out, its own title. Nothing else of the AI's copy of a locked step is used.
  - A locked step the answer left out goes back after the steps that came before it in the people's plan, but never before the answer's new tiny start: a plan of 3 or more steps must open with a start (§6.8).
  - `homework_ai_finish` marks every draft step `source` `'ai'`, the people's locked ones too (they carry their step's `id`), and cuts every step but a start or a turn-in to his session length, locked ones too. **Use this plan** brings a person's locked step back as that person's step: its own id, words, minutes and `'typed'` source (so it is locked again next time), never the draft's copy, and never by the draft's id alone (see the PR B review below).
  - Pat's view's `preview` (`tutor_view_by_token`) is the message without a request or answers: `plan.js` adds his request and answers as `private.ai_input` writes them (angle brackets replaced). They aren't scrubbed on the page: a name he types shows as typed there, and is sent as `[name]`. With neither, it is the message exactly (`tests/realdb/homework-ai.test.js` compares the two).
- **What Claude will see** opens when what would be sent differs from the last send from this browser: `plan.previewMark` is an FNV-1a hash of the typed parts (class, title, the teacher's text, the request, the answers, and each locked step's title and "done looks like" in the current plan), kept per assignment under `hh.ai.sent.<id>` in `localStorage` (and open every time where there is no storage). While a run is open the page reads the assignment every 5 seconds, for at most 6.5 minutes (the 6-minute lease, then the sweep).
- **The tests' places** differ from §14's list, so the two v1.3 tracks building at once don't edit the same files: the pages' AI parts are in **`tests/e2e/homework-ai.test.js`** (the Homework page, Pat's link and Jordan's line), not in `homework.test.js` and `day.test.js`; and the real-database checks are in **`tests/realdb/homework-ai.test.js`**, on its own copy of the database (its own household and clock), not in `flows.test.js` and `jobs.test.js`. Between them they cover §14's PR B list, including a Test while the switch is off. `mock.js` gains `aiRpc`, `aiJob`, `aiMessage` and `aiComplete`; `lib.js` the fake Anthropic (`h.anthropic`, `h.aiDone()`).
- **PR A's checks that changed, and why** (they described the pages before PR B): `homework.test.js`'s list of what the page reads gains `household` and `ai_runs` (the AI row), and the switches card's rows gain `ai` after the tutor links; `plan.test.js`'s list of `plan.js`'s exports gains `hashText` and `previewMark`, and "no AI part" is now checked without `opts.ai` rather than before PR B.
- `?v=` is `20261031d`: this release changes `plan.js`, `day.js`, the Homework page and their stylesheets.
- **The PR B review** (twelve findings, each reproduced first; the tests named fail on the code before it):
  - *A locked step came back as the scrubbed copy* ("Find 3 sources with [name] …" on his My day): `checkPlan` restores a locked step from `current_plan`, which is the scrubbed text the AI was sent, and Use this plan took the draft's words for any step whose id matched. Use this plan now takes a person's step from the plan itself (`plan.js` `aiUse`): a draft step matches a not-done typed step of the same kind whose words its copy could be (`copyOf`: `[name]`, `[link]`, `[email]`, `[phone]`, `[id]` stand for anything, `‹›` for `<>`), the one its id names first; it keeps that step's id, words and minutes and takes only the draft's day and place. A person's step renamed while the AI was asked keeps its new words (the copy's id names it), and one added meanwhile stays, before the turn-in: Use this plan never drops a person's typed step. The draft itself is unchanged (`hub-jobs` never reads a person's unscrubbed text). `homework-ai.test.js` (e2e and realdb: a step naming Pat, a link and `<`).
  - *Refs numbered twice* (a SQL gap in 0005): `private.ai_input` numbers the not-done steps when the run starts and `homework_ai_finish` numbers them again when it ends, so a tick, Make it smaller or a save in between moves a draft's ids one step on. The same matching handles it in PR B (a copy of a step done since is left out; a copy whose id names another step finds its own; one with no id finds its own); the root fix, the run keeping the ref-to-id map `ai_input` made, needs a migration and is for the next one. realdb `locked` ticks a step while the run is open.
  - *The fold stayed shut when a locked step changed*: `previewMark` now covers each locked step's title and "done looks like" (his renames on My day are typed), not the AI's or a starter plan's steps.
  - *What was sent wasn't tied to what was shown* (a school update or a note typed within the half second): Send and Ask again wait while a read is pending, read again at the tap and show a changed text instead of sending it; `shown` (plan.js's and homework.ts's `previewSeal`) lets hub-jobs end a run whose message differs as `changed`, with no call (§6.2, §6.4, §6.9). Pat's link reads the view again at the tap.
  - *A link split by a zero-width character survived and SQL joined it again*; 7-digit phone numbers weren't stripped: `checkPlan` takes the invisible characters out first, by code point, and its phone pattern's area code is optional (§6.8).
  - *Quadratic patterns on a long field*: a field over 1,200 characters, more steps than 12 and the locked ones, or more than 3 questions are `invalid` before any pattern runs (§6.8).
  - *Jordan's answers go without the questions they answer* (a SQL gap in `private.ai_input`, which never reads `ai_draft.questions`): confirmed, not fixed in PR B, which has no migration. The fix, each answer sent with its question by position (and the page sending answers by position), is for the next migration; until then the model reads the answers against the assignment and the plan.
  - *The switch vanished while the AI was on* without a Working test (the 90-day trim, a failed re-Test): it is drawn whenever the AI is on, so it can always be turned off.
  - *The list said "AI is drafting" for a run whose worker died*: the list reads the run's lease as the editor does.
  - *Test's line for a cap, the month or a refusal with a category* said "try again later": "Not run: AI help is used up for today (12 runs)…", "…for this month…", "the AI declined the test (cyber)".
  - *Any non-2xx from hub-jobs read as "Can't reach Home Hub"*: `plan.aiFailure` tells an answer (`FunctionsHttpError`: its body's stop, else `error`, "Try again later") from no answer (offline), for the editor and Test.
  - *Refusals Anthropic doesn't bill were counted*: `estimateCents` leaves out an attempt that declined before any output outside `bio`, `frontier_llm` and `reasoning_extraction` (§6.5).

**The coach view (0007)**
- **"His own" is the hub's model, day by day:** the person in charge, or nobody in charge and him as the only one on it, as `public.agenda(p_from, p_to)` has that day (so a one-day change counts); an event he is along on that someone else is in charge of is the family's, even when the title is about him. Google events count only when `private.google_for(p_person, p_from, p_to, p_grown_up)` puts them in his charge (his person calendar; the pets' or the house's when he is their owner), never the Family calendar's. "The next 14 and last 7 days" is today − 7 to today + 13, today counted among the 14, as Pat's week is 14 days from today.
- **His daily tasks include the days the daily job logged missed**, which `agenda` leaves out, so "done or not" covers a repeating task he didn't do; an open one shows on the day it is due (`private.current_due(t)`), as his My day has it.
- **Voids are their own kind (`voided`)**, beside the five kinds asked for, so the two weeks add up to the points that moved; a void isn't moved to the kind it corrects.
- **Points off makes `points`, `rewards` and `asks` null**, as his My day hides them: nothing to coach on while they're off.
- **Asks show their dates, not who decided**, and no parent's note with a decline; focus sessions show their day, never a clock time or with whom (the tutor view's "nothing live" and "never alone").
- **Notes**: at most 5 a day for a link (besides its 200 writes), kept 180 days (deleted by the link's next note), the page's key for a resend; the parents' page folds the done ones of the last 30 days. The coach sees its own notes of the last 30 days, with no word of whether a parent marked them done.
- **Jordan's screen says what Pat sees** (`me.coach` in his view, 0006's wrapper replaced): not asked for, but §0's rule is that his screen says who sees what, and with the coach view on its old line ("The grown-ups see your points") would be untrue.
- **The Coach section is day.js's**, in a box of its own under `plan.js`'s list (the brief names `day.js`), so `plan.js` and its tests are untouched; it is read after each read of the list, never on an assignment's screen.
- `?v=` is `20261031f` for this release (`20261031e` on its own branch, before the AI planner's `20261031d` went in first): it changes `day.js`, the Homework page and their stylesheets.

**The coach view, after its review (0007, edited in place: it is applied nowhere yet)**
- **Whose days they were.** `agenda` and `task_log` give a task's history and a series' past days to whoever is in charge now, which inside the family is how the hub has always worked; the coach view is the first place it reaches someone outside it, who judges him on the days he missed. So a chore rotated to him brought Sam's done, skipped and missed days, and Sam's past physio, with it. 0007 now records who was in charge (`task_log.responsible_id`, `tasks.responsible_changed_at`, `events.people_changed_at`, kept by triggers, §20.2) and the coach view shows a past day only when they say it was his (§20.3). Both of the review's options are used, each where it is exact: the entry's stamp for done and skipped (written when they happen), and the task's time for missed (the job logs a missed day only when the next one comes, after a hand-over that may have come between). On the day of a hand-over a missed day counts for neither (the safe side), and with a rotation back to him, his missed days from before the latest one are left out too. A series' day counts from the instant of the change (the 4pm physio before a 5:30pm hand-over was Sam's), unless that day's own change named him. Nothing from before 0007 is taken for his: its entries have no stamp, and the tasks and events already there take 0007's time, as a column default dropped at once, so no row is saved (no `updated_at` moves, no calendar write is due).
- **Done one-offs need their tick's entry** (stamped), where they used to come from `tasks.done_at` alone; `complete_task` always writes one. `coach.test.sql`'s household now has those entries for its two done one-offs, as the pages would have written them; its expectations are unchanged.
- **Health tasks are "Private item"** in the coach view, whatever their own Private switch, as on the wall (0003's `calendar_desired`, v1.1 rule 7b) and in the switch's words: the coach link is an audience outside the family. Events have no category, so this is the tasks' (§20.8 6).
- **Late tasks**: his open tasks due before the last 7 days are in his daily tasks (status `late`, first, oldest first, "Late since Thu Oct 22"), as his My day's Late lists them; the ones due inside the 7 days already showed on their day.
- **Asks decided in the last 30 days** show however long ago he asked, newest first by the later date, so an old ask approved yesterday is there beside its −500 trade line, as on his own view.
- **A note's key is tied to its words**: after "Can't reach Home Hub", sending the same words again finds the note; changed words get a new key, so a corrected note isn't answered `changed: false` and dropped while the page says "Sent to the family.". If the first one had landed, the parents see both.
- **Jordan's who-sees line** names everything the coach view shows (his points and rewards, focus sessions, own calendar and daily tasks), not only the points.

**Joining the AI planner and the coach view (PR B first, then 0007 on top)**
- The two were built side by side from `main` at `e283b14`; PR B goes in first, and the coach view's branch takes `main` with PR B in before it does. In the database nothing meets: PR B has no migration, and 0007's wrapper of the tutor's view already answers `p_part 'ai'` exactly as 0005's (§20.4), so the AI's 5-second reads carry no `coach` and ask for no coach view.
- **Pat's link** (`day.js`): the coach view's two boxes stay (`plan.js`'s tutor view draws into `.tutor-main`, the Coach section is `.coach-main`), and the tutor view gets PR B's `sent` marks and `api.ai` (hub-jobs' `homework_ai`, the link's token in the body); a link that stops working sets the title back and turns the Coach section off. The Coach section is still the list's only, so on an assignment the AI's part of the editor is as PR B has it, and back on the list the Coach section is read again.
- **The parents' page** (`homework.js`): each tutor link has **Can ask the AI** (PR B, `save_tutor_link`) and under it, on schema 7, **Coach view** (`set_tutor_coach`) with the coach's notes; each is its own write and neither changes the other (0007 leaves `save_tutor_link` as 0005 has it, and it never touches `coach`). The `change` handler has both actions, and `homework.css` both sets of rules.
- **Counts and lists**: anon still calls the twenty-two token functions of 0007 (PR B adds none); `CLAUDE.md`'s line for `day.html` names them all and, in the tutor link's mode only, `functions.invoke('hub-jobs')` for the AI planner, as v2-design's tutor link now does too. PR B adds no owner decision, so the coach view's stays 16. The README keeps both sections (the AI planner's, then the coach view's) and both lines under "What Pat sees" and "What goes where".
- **Tests**: `mock.js` exports both sets of fakes (`withCoach`, `coachRpc`, `coachNoteRow` and `aiRpc`, `aiJob`, `aiMessage`, `aiComplete`); `tests/README.md` lists `homework-ai.test.js` after `homework.test.js`, with the coach view's additions to `homework.test.js`, `day.test.js` and `tutor.test.js` kept, and both real-database notes. `homework-ai.test.js` gains section 4b, the two on one link: the parents' row on schema 7 (Can ask the AI, then Coach view, each saved without changing the other) and Pat's link with both on ("AI draft ready" and the Coach section on the list, Send to Claude on the assignment with no coach view read, the Coach section read again back on the list, only the tutor and coach functions and `hub-jobs` called). The real-database template now has 0007, so `tests/realdb/homework-ai.test.js` runs on schema 7.
- `?v=` is `20261031f` on every script and stylesheet of both pages, and in `core-v2.test.js`: one new value for the joined release (`20261031d` was PR B's and `20261031e` the coach view's on its own).

**The first-import check when nothing is due yet (0008)**
- The owner's fix for a link read over a weekend or holiday (every item past, so the check was empty and the link couldn't be answered): the check fills with the latest past items, each with `past`, in date order. The ones to come still come first; with 5 or more to come the answer is 0006's with `past: false` on each.
- **Past items are any not gone, however old** (the owner's rule: "the most recent past ones"). Since the reader lets go of past items nobody made an assignment once they are 14 days old, the older ones a check can show are assignments made from the link before a Remove; they are past their date like the rest and come last.
- **Removed or checked: no check**, as before, and an item made an assignment can still be on it, as on 0006 (the check never looked at `made_at`).
- **The undo re-creates 0006's function** rather than refusing to run again on a database set back to 7 (as 0006 and 0007 do for theirs): 0008 adds no table to tell a set-back database by, and replacing the status card twice is harmless. `tests/db/run.sh` runs the undo and then 0008 again.
- Version 8 is a marker only: no page asks for anything new on it, and the Homework page reads the check the same way on 7 and 8. `access.test.sql`, `coach.test.sql` and `flows.test.js` pin 8 now.
- **The page** (`homework.js`, `schoolCheckHTML`; no version gate): an item with `past: true` reads "was due Tue Nov 3" (muted, `.hw-school-past`) instead of "due …"; with any past item the lead line is "Nothing is due yet, so these are the latest ones." (every item past) or "Only 2 are still to come, so the latest past ones fill in.", then "Are these the dates Blackbaud shows? It still lists past assignments." and the line that until a parent answers nothing from the Assignments link reaches Jordan or Pat (their first names from the page, as before). With nothing past it is 0006's "Is this what Blackbaud shows?". The buttons and `check_school_feed` are unchanged; only the toast after an all-past check says "Dates checked. Jordan's New from school shows the next ones once the school posts them." (instead of "…shows them now.", which would be false: nothing past reaches New from school).
- **An empty check says why**, from the Assignments link's own status, so it reads right on 7 (no `past` key; `[]` when every item is past) and on 8: no `last_ok_at`: the old words, unchanged ("Once the Assignments link is read, its next 5 items show here, to check against Blackbaud."); `last_count` 0: "The Assignments link was read today 3:10pm and held no assignments due from 2 weeks ago on. The next ones show here once the school posts some, to check against Blackbaud."; `last_count` above 0 (on a database before 0008; and, until its next read, a link removed and added again on one, which 0006's Add link leaves with the old link's last read): "The Assignments link was read today 3:10pm: all 6 of its assignments are already past. The next ones show here once the school posts one due today or later, to check against Blackbaud." ("its one assignment is" for 1). Each ends "Until you answer, nothing from it reaches Jordan or Pat."
- **Tests**: `mock.js`'s `withSchoolCheck(db)` makes the fake status card answer 0008's check (without it, 0006's, as on 6 and 7). `homework.test.js` section 11: on schema 7 the three empty checks (not read, Check now reading nothing, every item past, and one); on schema 8 every item past (the five latest, "was due", the lead line, Looks right with the same `check_school_feed` and the new toast), two to come and six past (One day off, A day later), one to come, seven to come (exactly as before), not read and read empty, and a phone. `tests/realdb/school.test.js`'s all-past flow checks the "was due" lines, the lead line and the toast on the real database.
- `?v=` is `20261031g` on every script and stylesheet of both pages, and in `core-v2.test.js` (the page and `homework.css` changed).
- **After the review** (three findings, all minor, all fixed):
  - **A link removed and added again kept the old link's last read.** 0006's Remove keeps the row (for the assignments made from it) with its `last_try_at`, `last_ok_at`, `last_count` and `last_repeats`, and Add link's on-conflict update kept them too. So until the new link's first read, the row said "Last read … · 6 items", and an empty check (every item kept since gone from the school site) said "…all 6 of its assignments are already past", about items Remove had deleted, some of them still to come. The page can't tell that row from a real read (nothing on the status card says when a link was added again), so 0008 also replaces `set_school_feed`: added again, a removed row starts as not read (the four columns cleared; a live link's Replace keeps them). The clearing happens at Add, not at Remove, so a link removed before 0008 is mended too when it is added again after it. Two side effects, both intended: Check now reads such a link at once even within a minute of the old link's last read (it skips a link tried in the last minute); and the first read after it doesn't follow a kept item without a UID whose date moved while the link was removed (the moved-item step looks only at what the link's last good read saw, and there is none yet): it comes as a new item, and the kept one is marked gone after two reads. On a database before 0008 the old words still show for that row until its next read.
  - **The toast after an answer** was chosen from the check before it ("every item past"), but the answer re-dates every item: **A day later** makes an item due yesterday due today, so it is on his New from school while the toast said it wasn't, and **A day earlier** on one due today did the opposite (that one older than 0008). It is now chosen from `check_school_feed`'s answer (its `new`), with the status read again after the write standing in if the answer is missing.
  - **A paused link, or one stopped on something only Replace mends**, isn't read (or isn't read with any luck), so "The next ones show here once the school posts some" was a false promise. The end of the read-link texts now says to resume it first, or to copy the link again and use Replace first. Stops Home Hub tries again (`unreachable`, `timeout`, `down`, `http`, `too_big`, `too_many`, `empty`, `error`) keep the old words, and so does the not-read text, which promises nothing.
  - Tests: `school.test.sql` follows Riley's link on (Remove kept it with its last read; Add again: not read, its check the item made homework; Check now hands it at once; two reads take that item off the site; Replace keeps the new link's last read; Remove and Add again with every item kept gone: no check, not read); `run.sh` refuses 0008 on a database at 0007 without `set_school_feed` or `private.school_feed_urls`, and its undo puts back both functions; `access.test.sql` checks `set_school_feed`'s rules and grants. `homework.test.js` section 11: A day later on an all-past check (toast "shows them now", New (1)), A day earlier on 7 (the other toast), paused on 8 and on 7 (and Resume and Pause changing the words), `gone` and `sign_in`, every stop's ending, and Remove then Add link again on 8 (`mock.js`'s `set_school_feed` clears the old read under `withSchoolCheck`, as 0008 does). `tests/realdb/school.test.js` checks the row added again has no old read and says "Added · first read within the hour".

**Timelines (§21, no migration)**
- **Pat's Next 3 weeks is inside the Assignments section**, at its top under the heading "Next 3 weeks", not a section of its own, so the link's fixed order (§9.5) and its tests stay as they were. `plan.js`'s tutor view draws it and the chart (the link's screens are its), so `day.js` only names them in its header.
- **Pat's chart sits between the fields and the editor** ("above the editor"), in a card of its own, and is drawn again after every read and save, but only when it changed, so a chart scrolled inside its box stays put (as on the parents' page).
- **His heading is "Your path"** ("Tu camino"), each card named by the assignment's title with "›", a link to `#plan/<id>`; the rest fold under "+N more" ("+N más"), which stays open through reads. On `#plan/<id>` the path is drawn for any plan given to him (not only long ones): it is the one he opened. It follows the plan as last read or saved, even under unsaved changes in the editor.
- **No points on his path** (§21.4): his view doesn't carry a milestone's value; left out rather than guessed.
- **Flags he won before today** sit at the start of the path, level with the dots.
- `?v=` stays `20261031h`, the value the parents' part of this release set on every script and stylesheet of both pages.
- **From the timelines' review** (each with a test that failed before): a chart longer than 63 days keeps today and what's ahead, and a step outside its days is never drawn on another day (one past the cut was squeezed into the last column, on a day that wasn't its own); a done or dropped assignment's unticked steps aren't "from earlier"; a plan whose first step is past the 3 weeks has no bar in them (it had a one-day stub on their last day); his path names a passed flag's day by its date ("from Fri Oct 30", not "from Fri", which read as the Friday his dots show) and says "Do by" for a lesson; a chart wider than its box opens on what's next, with its names in view, an edge shade and keyboard scrolling (`tlScroll`, on both pages); "Today" on his path keeps its width beside four won flags; a long class wraps inside the overview's name column. The tests: the clock-change drawings again in New York (Kiritimati never changes its clocks, so a day counted in local time passed there), the parents' page's requests checked one by one, and the path's redraw from a Save's and a Re-plan's answer.
